## Story

**Added by Lionel, 2026-10-20 (release blocker):** "A: Before 2.3.0 (Rec.)". The question he answered, word for word, after the PO's sweep (orchestrator's decision log, "Release scope: accents stored two ways (NFD)"): "Fix the two-ways-of-storing-accents problem before releasing 2.3.0, or after?" The options, word for word: "A: Before 2.3.0 (Rec.)" — "One new story converts every file read, every typed name and every saved name to the usual form. All the cases above then behave like typed text. About one more pipeline round and one more Codex review." / "B: After 2.3.0" — "For 2.3.0, only what got worse is fixed: the « Mme Zo » detection case and the new checks in 10.16, 10.17, 10.19. The old cases wait for their own story after the release." Option A's preview, as the decision log records it, listed: every file read (.txt .docx .pdf .xlsx .csv) converted to the usual accent form; every typed and saved name compared in that form; fixes missed names, « Mme Zo », corrections, [A] not found, second pseudonym, deletion; written files then use the usual form (same look, different bytes); one accuracy run must show the scores unchanged; 10.17 and 10.19 build on it; cost one more story, one more Codex review. Found by the read-only Codex review of 10.16 (2026-10-20) for one check, then measured by the PO across the release paths (Context).

**As a** user whose documents may store an accented letter as the plain letter followed by a separate accent mark (some PDFs and some programs write it so),
**I want** every file read, every name I type and every comparison with my saved names to use one form,
**so that** such a name is found, replaced, reused and erased exactly like the same name written the usual way, and no part of it is left in clear.

**Priority:** HIGH — release blocker for v2.3.0 (Lionel, 2026-10-20). Paths 1, 2, 3, 5 and 6 leave a name or part of it in clear; paths 4 and 7 give one person two pseudonyms; path 8 keeps a saved pseudonym after an erasure that says it is gone (Context).
**Change type:** one composed form (Unicode NFC) at every entry: the readers (`utils/file_handler.py`, `utils/tabular_reader.py`), the typed texts (CLI prompts and arguments, desktop dialogs) and the comparisons with saved names (`data/repositories/mapping_repository.py` and its callers); the product's own word cuts keep combining marks inside their words (AC4); written files come out composed (AC5). The detector's code and patterns (`nlp/`, `resources/`) do not change: they receive composed text. G1 applies as a check (AC7): one accuracy run, its 16 lines unchanged. No file under `tests/accuracy/` or `tests/test_corpus/` changes.
**Ordering:** outside the detector chain. After 10.16 merges, rebased on it: 10.16 composes the texts its checks cut before cutting them (`document_words` and the pseudonym's runs; Decisions Recorded, item 2) and keeps `_WORD = \w+` (`core/custom_pseudonym.py:267` at `43041e9`); 10.20 changes the word cutting itself (AC4), next to 10.16's lines in the same file. Before 10.17 and 10.19, which build their places and forms on its composed text: each rebases on 10.20 and adds a decomposed-accent guard case to its own tests (a decomposed document and a composed typed name: every place reached, never an "Added" while a place stays in clear; path 9). It shares `validation/models.py` (`places_of`, `is_listed_place`) and `validation/workflow.py` (`_locate_modified_span`, the typed prompts) with 10.19; `cli/commands/delete_mapping.py` and `data/repositories/mapping_repository.py` with 10.19; `core/document_processor.py` with 10.16; and it may share `gui/screens/validation.py` and `gui/models/validation_state.py` with 10.16, 10.17 and 10.19. 10.12, 10.13, 10.14, 10.15 and 10.18 are merged (`main` `43041e9`). Its own PR; stories that change the same file are sequenced and the later one is rebased on the earlier, never merged together. Every story edits `CHANGELOG.md`. Before the v2.3.0 release.
**Epic:** [Epic 10: v2.3 — Detection Precision Quick Wins](../prd/epic-10-v2.3-detection-precision-quick-wins.md) (story 10.20)
**Branch:** `story/10.20-accents-one-form`, on the PM's epic commits `44b695c` (the epic's 10.20 section), `bedf375` (10.20 in the duration, the open stories, the Execution Sequence, the compatibility line, the risks, the done list and the status) and `8bd21c0` (the 10.20 section after the PO's review of v1.0: AC2, AC3, AC4, AC6, AC8, the Context, Open Points (a) and (c), the Ordering, the Integration Points), `5b10f2f` (Lionel's Qa, Qb and Qc closing Open Points (a) and (b), AC9 and the compatibility line), with the SM's v1.0 (`9842a18`) between them, on `main` `43041e9` (10.12 to 10.15 and 10.18 merged). This story's commits change `docs/stories/` only. 10.16 has not merged yet: this story rebases on it before development (Ordering).
**Baseline:** the behaviour is measured on `43041e9` (the SM's sketch and probes, Dev Notes "Reproduction Evidence"); the accuracy check is AC7's run, its 16 lines identical to `38212303870`.
**Code authorship:** the BMAD dev agent (Claude) writes the tests first, then the fix (AC6, AC10; Lionel, 2026-10-18: Codex reviews, never writes). Before the merge command, the orchestrator runs one read-only `codex exec` review of the PR's final head, and runs it again only if the code changes after it. If Codex cannot run, the work stops and Lionel is told. The SM's prototype, sketch and probes are Claude-written measurement files: never committed, never applied in the worktree (Dev Notes "Environment").

### Context

Copied word for word from the epic at `106366e` (the Context after the PM's P0-1 edit, `315eb24`):

At `main` `43041e9`. Measured by the PO on 2026-10-20, read-only, on `main` and on v2.2.0 (probes `rgpd-analysis/r1019/po/nfd/probe/test_nfd_*.py`, results in `r1019/po/nfd/logs/`; invented names only; detection replaced except in path 1). "Decomposed" means an accented letter stored as its plain letter followed by a separate accent mark (« o » + U+0301, Unicode NFD); "composed" means the usual single accented letter (« ó », Unicode NFC). Both look the same on screen; the product compares them as different texts. Every path was run with the same invented text written both ways.

- **Where it starts:** no product code converts text to one form when a document is read or a name is typed; `unicodedata` only folds accents inside two comparisons (`core/custom_pseudonym.py:196-197`, `nlp/hybrid_detector.py:298`). A combining mark is not a word character (`\w`), so the word patterns cut a decomposed word at its mark: the 10.16 check (`core/custom_pseudonym.py:267`), 10.15's places of [A] (`validation/models.py:32-33`), and the detector's title pattern (`resources/detection_patterns.yaml:15`).
- **The nine paths (the PO's table, in short; decomposed text, name typed composed where a name is typed):**

  | # | Path | `main` `43041e9` | v2.2.0 |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | Detection, the real detector on 10 invented sentences | 8 of the 10 sentences are detected differently: names missed (« Monsieur Zórbalia Quentrel », « Maître Éloria Quentrel », « Madame Éloria Vórlan »), shortened (« Vorlan Zórbalia » to « Zórbalia ») or given another type (« Éloria Vórlan » as ORG). **New:** in 2 of them « Mme Zórbalia » is cut to « Mme Zo », because the title pattern stops at the mark; the written file reads « Mme Mina Meyer », the mark, then « rbalia »: the end of the name in clear | The same misses; « Mme Zo » was found too, but spaCy also found the whole « Mme Zórbalia », and nothing of it stayed in clear |
  | 2 | CLI [A] (10.15) | « ⚠️ 'Zórbalia' not found in document », both places in clear, nothing added. In a document holding both forms, only the places written like the typed name are replaced | The same « not found » (and one place only) |
  | 3 | CLI [E] (10.15) | « Gilles Robin Quentrel a signé. Signé : Gilles Robin Quentrel. »: « Quentrel » in clear at both places; the row is stored composed | The same |
  | 4 | CLI review grouping, 10.13's count | The same name written composed at one place and decomposed at another is shown as separate names (3 unique instead of 1); accepted, the file reads « Fionn Salgado a signé. Signé : Jade Salgado. »: two pseudonyms for one person in one document, two rows | The same |
  | 5 | Desktop, single-document review | A correction typed composed on a decomposed name leaves « Quentrel » in clear at both places. In a document holding both forms, the decomposed row is not reached and stays to review; accepted as it is, the file keeps « Quentrel », and two rows are stored. A correction that extends the dialog's own text leaves nothing in clear. A name added by hand: no difference between the forms (one place only, the leak 10.17 fixes) | Not measured |
  | 6 | Desktop, batch review | The same as path 5 for corrections | Not measured |
  | 7 | A saved name reused in a later document (`process` twice; `batch --workers 1`) | Saved from a composed document and met decomposed, or the reverse: a second pseudonym (« Mona Andrade », then « André Andrade ») and a second row | The same |
  | 8 | `delete-mapping` by name | Saved decomposed: « Entity Not Found », exit 1, the saved pseudonym stays. Saved both ways: only the composed one is deleted, while the screen says « The pseudonym '…' is now permanently anonymous. ». `list-entities --search` with the composed name does not list the decomposed one (the probe's line count) | The same |
  | 9 | The 10.17 and 10.19 prototypes (not merged) | The forms rule finds only « ZORBALIA » (written without accent), so [A] shows « ℹ️ Added PERSON entity: Zórbalia » and « Added: 1 » while « Zórbalia » and « ZÓRBALIA », written decomposed, stay in clear: `main`'s visible « not found » becomes a silent "Added". 10.19's erasure across capitals, typed composed, leaves both decomposed saved pseudonyms | Not in v2.2.0 |

- **The 10.16 check (Codex, 2026-10-20):** on 10.16's head `463481a`, the custom pseudonym « Xantor Zórbalia » is accepted for a reviewed name « quentrel zórbalia » written decomposed, at set time and at finalization, and the real surname is written: the check cuts « zo » and « rbalia » apart at the mark before it folds accents (Codex P0, `rgpd-analysis/r1016/codex/review_final.txt`). 10.16 fixes it inside its checks (Decisions Recorded, item 2).
- **Two letters of a name in clear, and names saved wrongly (found by the SM, reproduced by the PO on a fresh database):** the title pattern (`utils/french_patterns.py:18`) ends a title where the next character is not a word character, and an accent mark is not one. So « Mélora » written decomposed begins with the title « Me »: the written file keeps « Me » before the pseudonym (« Le contrat avec MeEytan Roger a signé. » for « Le contrat avec Mélora Quentrel a signé. »), and the name is saved as « lora Quentrel » after a lone accent mark; a name ending « …ôme » loses « me » the same way (5 of the 106 accented names of the app's name lists). A decomposed « Maître » is not recognised as a title, so the name after it is saved with « Maître » in its key; a LOCATION after a decomposed « à » keeps « à » in its key the same way. The same in v2.2.0 and at `43041e9`. Fixed here by AC1, since the pattern then reads composed text; the rows saved so are covered by AC3.
- **New or pre-existing:** paths 1 (the misses), 2, 3, 4, 7 and 8 behave the same in v2.2.0; paths 5 and 6 were measured on `main` only. New since v2.2.0, never released: the « Mme Zo » cut (path 1), the 10.16 check (fixed in 10.16) and the prototypes' silent "Added" (path 9), which must not merge so (Ordering).
- **Where decomposed text comes from (the PO's inputs probe):** `.txt`, `.csv`, `.xlsx` and `.docx` return what the file stores, composed or decomposed. Typing in Word gives composed text; a decomposed paragraph that a script types into Word stays decomposed in the `.docx`; Word's « Save as PDF » gives composed text, even for that paragraph. A PDF that draws the accent apart from its letter (the PO made one with fpdf2) is returned decomposed: pdfplumber gives the accent as its own character (`utils/file_handler.py:114-116`), and its `unicode_norm="NFC"` option does not join it to its letter, because it normalizes each character alone (measured by the SM and the PO: 4 marks left). So `read_pdf` composes the text it extracts (AC1). Typed names are what the keyboard or the paste gives. How often real documents hold decomposed accents is not measured.
- **The PO's recommendation, which option A follows:** one point for the composed form, rather than a check in each story: the readers (for PDFs, the extracted text, since pdfplumber's `unicode_norm` does not compose it), the typed texts, and both sides of every comparison with saved names. The cost: a file written decomposed comes out composed (same look, different bytes), and one accuracy run.
- **The accuracy suite** loads 13 product files (the 10.11 count: `gdpr_pseudonymizer/__init__.py`, eight files of `nlp/`, `resources/__init__.py`, `utils/__init__.py`, `utils/french_patterns.py`, `utils/logger.py`), none of the readers. The run of AC7 is the check Lionel's option A asks for: the scores unchanged.
- **Not in this story:** compatibility forms (Unicode NFKC: ligatures such as « fi » stored as one character, full-width letters, …), which change more than accents, unless the SM measures a need; the detector's own patterns (Open Points (c)).

**State at `43041e9` (SM re-read and measured, 2026-10-20; details in Dev Notes "Code Map", "Citation Check", "Reproduction Evidence", "Open Points (a) and (b), measured" and "Consumer Trace"):**

- **Every citation of the epic holds at `43041e9`** (Citation Check). The worktree's product code is `43041e9`'s (`git diff --stat 43041e9 8bd21c0 -- gdpr_pseudonymizer tests` is empty).
- **The SM's sketch of the Test Plan** (Dev Notes "Test Plan"; 57 tests, v1.1): on a fresh `git archive 43041e9`, 50 tests fail with an `AssertionError` on their first assertion after their preconditions, and 6 guards pass; the spaCy-marked « Mme Zo » test fails too. On the SM's prototype v1.1 (Fix Design, option 1 of Open Point (a)) all 57 pass. The same in one process and under `-n 4`. The existing suites give the same result on both trees: no existing test changes at `43041e9` (Reproduction Evidence, item 4).
- **« Mme Zo », with the real detector, through the product's reader** (the PO's ten sentences and the PO's path-1 text, each written to a `.txt` decomposed and composed): on `43041e9`, 9 of the 11 decomposed texts are detected differently from their composed copy, and 3 hold the « Mme Zo » cut; on the prototype, all 11 are read composed and detected exactly as their composed copy, and no span is cut at a mark. One honest limit: on the PO's path-1 text, `43041e9`'s decomposed copy found « Zórbalia Quentrel » twice where the composed copy misses it; after the fix the decomposed copy behaves like the composed one, misses included (Notes for the PO and PM, item 3).
- **The PDF reader:** pdfplumber's `unicode_norm="NFC"` does not join a letter and an accent drawn as two characters (it normalizes each character alone; measured on a hand-made PDF and on the PO's fpdf2 way). Only composing the extracted text does (Fix Design, AC1).
- **The title and preposition patterns on decomposed text (the epic's Context, "Two letters of a name in clear, and names saved wrongly"), measured on fresh databases:** v2.2.0 and `43041e9` both write « Le contrat avec Me<pseudonym> a signé. » for a decomposed « Le contrat avec Mélora Quentrel a signé. » (v2.2.0 « MeEytan Roger », « MeLéa Simões »; `43041e9` « MeLou Roque », the PO's, and « MeAda Cristiano », the SM's T26), and save the key « lora Quentrel » after a lone U+0301; a decomposed « Maître Vorlan Mestrix » or « Maître Éloria Quentrel » is saved with « Maître » (decomposed) in its key, and a place after a decomposed « à » keeps « à » the same way. 5 of the 106 accented names of the app's own name list lose letters (v1.8: 106, not 111; five names are both first names and surnames and were counted twice). The prototype v1.1 writes « Le contrat avec <pseudonym> a signé. » and saves « Mélora Quentrel » composed (T26), and reaches every such old row from the composed name, with or without its title: on the PO's eight old databases (v2.2.0 and `43041e9`; « Mélora », « Maître Éloria », « Maître Vorlan Mestrix », « Jérôme »-like), the old pseudonym is reused and `delete-mapping` with the bare name erases the row, 16 of 16 (v1.0's prototype missed the four « Maître » rows: the PO's M1). Open Points (a) and (b), measured.
- **Open Point (a), measured** (databases of 1,009 and 10,087 invented rows, one accented name in five saved decomposed, one in fifty saved both ways): option 1 (one indexed query for the composed name and the keys an earlier version could have saved, « Maître » and « à » included) costs 500 lookups 0.150 s and 0.153 s against 0.110 s and 0.115 s today (v1.1's quieter run, the numbers Qa was asked with; side by side in one process on a busier machine, 0.162 s and 0.209 s against 0.111 s and 0.149 s; about 0.1 ms more per lookup), and finds all 500 names (today 457 and 471); option 1 by a scan (decrypt every row and compare) costs 0.038 s and 0.601 s per scan, and misses the rows saved wrongly; option 2 (rewrite once) costs 0.069 s and 0.425 s, once, leaves 9 and 87 names saved both ways as they are, and cannot repair the rows saved wrongly.
- **Open Points (a) and (b) are decided** (Lionel, 2026-10-21; Decisions Recorded): option 1 and rule A, below as measured before he chose.
- **Open Point (b), measured on databases v2.2.0 wrote (the PO's path 7):** a composed then a decomposed document give two rows and two pseudonyms (« Anya Branco », « Lise Branco »); the reverse order gives « Noé Fonseca », « Nassim Fonseca ». On `43041e9` each form keeps its own pseudonym and `delete-mapping` erases only the composed row. On the prototype both forms get one pseudonym (the composed row's, or the oldest with the other rule) and `delete-mapping` lists and erases both, each with its ERASURE entry.
- **AC9, measured, then decided:** with a name saved both ways, the prototype v1.0's screen showed « You are about to delete the following 2 entity mappings (one per type of this name): » above two « Name: Zórbalia Quentrel », both « Type: PERSON »: false. Lionel chose the line « You are about to delete the following N saved pseudonyms (all of this name, whatever its type): » (Qc); the prototype v1.2 shows it (T30).
- **Paths 5 and 6 on v2.2.0, measured (AC8):** a correction typed composed on a decomposed name leaves « Quentrel » in clear at the corrected place, in the single-document review and in the batch review, as on `43041e9` (v2.2.0's other places stay in clear for its own older reasons, fixed by 10.9 and 10.10). So the CHANGELOG may say "already in v2.2.0" for paths 5 and 6 too.

## Acceptance Criteria

AC1 to AC11 are copied from the epic's Story 10.20 at `8ee43b0` word for word (AC3 and AC8 after Lionel's P0-1, `315eb24`; AC3, AC6 and AC8 after his end-cut answer, `0a531a0` and `9f0ccd5`; AC3's import sentence after QA's IMPORT-002, `8ee43b0`). "AC precisions (story-level)" adds detail and does not change the epic text. AC12 to AC15 are story-level.

1. **AC1 — Every reader returns composed text (Lionel, 2026-10-20, option A):** `read_file` for `.txt` and `.md`, `read_docx`, `read_pdf` (pdfplumber), `read_excel` and `read_csv`, and the structured readers `read_excel_structured` and `read_csv_structured` (every cell) return their text in composed form (Unicode NFC). Every path that reads a document goes through them, the CLI (`process`, `batch` one file at a time and parallel) and the desktop app (single document and batch); the SM lists any reader or path that does not, and covers it. So the detector, the review (its text, its highlights, a name selected for an add by hand) and every later step see one form. Compatibility forms (NFKC) are not changed (Context, "Not in this story").
2. **AC2 — Every typed name is read in composed form:** in the CLI, the texts typed at [A] ('Entity text'), [E] ('Enter corrected entity text') and [C] ('Enter custom pseudonym'), the name given to `delete-mapping`, and the `--search` text of `list-entities` and `list-mappings`; in the desktop app, the texts typed in « Modifier le texte de l'entité » and « Changer le pseudonyme », in the review's « Filtrer les entités... » field and in the database screen's search field. The SM lists any other place where the user types a name, and covers it.
3. **AC3 — Saved names are compared in composed form on both sides:** every comparison of a text with saved names compares composed forms: the reuse of a saved name and its pseudonym (`find_by_full_name_and_type`, `find_all_by_full_name`, the library's `find_by_component`, `import-mappings`' duplicate check), the erasure (`delete-mapping` by name removes every saved pseudonym whose name has the same composed form, listed before the confirmation, each with its 10.12 erasure entry) and the search (`list-entities`, `list-mappings`, the desktop database search). So a database written by v2.2.0 or by today's code still matches, with both kinds of old row: rows whose name kept its decomposed letters, and rows an earlier version saved wrongly because a title or preposition pattern met a decomposed name (Context): « Mélora Quentrel » saved as « lora Quentrel » after a lone accent mark, a name written after a decomposed « Maître » saved with « Maître » in its key, and a LOCATION saved with « à » before the place by the same mechanism. Rows the app saves are composed; rows `import-mappings` brings in are copied exactly as they were saved, because composing can erase the mark that shows an earlier version cut the name (QA, 2026-10-22, IMPORT-002). No saved row is rewritten: every lookup by name also tries the ways an earlier version saved the name (Lionel, 2026-10-21, Qa "A: Find as they are (Rec.)"). Rows an earlier version saved wrongly are reached when the loss of letters left a sign (a word starting with a lone accent mark, as « lora Quentrel »; two words glued where a space was, as « JérôQuentrel » for « Jérôme Quentrel »), and so are the rows with « Maître » or « à » kept in the key. A name that lost its last letters (« Jérôme » written alone, saved as « Jérô ») is not reached by its full name: that person gets a second pseudonym in later documents, as in v2.2.0, and `delete-mapping` with the full name finds nothing, while typing what was saved (« Jérô ») still erases it (Lionel, 2026-10-22, P0-1 "A: Only with a sign (Rec.)"). The P0-1 question as asked, word for word (the full text he was shown is in the story): "10.20 P0-1: names an earlier version saved with their last letters missing. Find them only when the loss left a sign, or all of them?" The options: "A: Only with a sign (Rec.)" — "Removes one of the two wrong-person cases. A name cut at its very end (« Jérô ») gets a second pseudonym, as in 2.2.0. The other case can't be closed without losing far more common names like « André »." / "B: All of them" — "Every cut name is still found by its full name, the last-letter ones too, and both wrong-person cases stay." The check that refuses a custom pseudonym equal to a real name already saved (10.11's Q-C2, at set time and at finalization) compares with the repaired forms of old rows, including what a name cut at its end could have been: « Jérôme » is refused as a pseudonym when « Jérô » was saved by an earlier version. It only ever refuses, so a few made-up pseudonyms are refused too (« Vorlan Zorbôme » when « Vorlan Zorbô » is saved); finding, reusing and erasing stay as P0-1 decided (Lionel, 2026-10-22, end cut "A: Refuse them too (Rec.)"). The end-cut question as asked, word for word (the full text he was shown is in the orchestrator's questions file, section "End cut"): "10.20 end cut: should the pseudonym check also refuse what a cut name could have been?" The options: "A: Refuse them too (Rec.)" — "« Jérôme » is refused as a pseudonym when « Jérô » was saved. Wrong outcome: a few made-up pseudonyms are refused too, and you choose another. Finding, reusing, erasing stay as in P0-1." / "B: Keep it as it is" — "The check ignores cut names. Wrong outcome: « Jérôme » can be chosen as a pseudonym when « Jérô » was saved, and that real name is written in the document." How old rows are reached: the exact lookups (reuse, erasure, `import-mappings`' duplicate check) try the keys an earlier version could have saved; the comparisons that scan the saved rows (the saved-real-name check and the four searches: `list-entities`, `list-mappings`, the desktop database screen and its worker) rebuild from each saved row the texts it may stand for, and only add them to what is compared. When a name and type are saved both ways, later documents use the pseudonym of the row saved the usual way, and both rows stay saved (Lionel, 2026-10-21, Qb "A: Usual way's one (Rec.)"). Open Points (a) and (b) record both decisions.
4. **AC4 — A word keeps its combining marks (the 10.16 SM's follow-up (f)):** wherever the product cuts text into words or checks that a place is a whole word, outside the detector, a combining mark (every character for which `unicodedata.combining` is not 0) stays inside its word, also where Unicode has no composed letter for it (« x » + U+0301). This covers at least the 10.16 checks (`core/custom_pseudonym.py`, `_WORD`, as 10.16 leaves it), 10.15's places of [A] (`validation/models.py`, `_NOT_AFTER_WORD` and `_NOT_BEFORE_WORD`, `places_of`, `is_listed_place`), the correction locator (`validation/workflow.py`, `_locate_modified_span`) and the redaction of names in logged and audited error messages (`core/document_processor.py`, `_sanitize_error_message`, its name pattern `:1079`); the SM lists every other cut it finds. So a decomposed « zórbalia » is one word, and « Zo » is never a whole-word place inside it. The detector's patterns are not changed here (Open Points (c)).
5. **AC5 — Written files use the composed form (Lionel's option A: "same look, different bytes"):** the written text file and every cell written back to an `.xlsx` or `.csv` file hold composed text, also where no name was replaced, since they are written from what the readers return; the rows saved from them hold composed text. The CHANGELOG says so (AC8).
6. **AC6 — Tests first, invented names only:** the BMAD dev agent writes the tests and commits them before the fix. Each of the PO's nine paths gets a test with the same invented text written composed and decomposed (and with both forms in one document where the PO measured it), through the real readers and the real CLI or desktop paths, detection replaced except for the « Mme Zo » case. Each expected failure is an `assert` on the written file, the stored rows or the line shown, made after the test's preconditions hold, and each failing test is marked `xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)` on the old code (the 10.5 pattern). The fix removes the markers. Cases:
   - **path 1, « Mme Zo »:** a ".txt" holding « Mme Zórbalia » written decomposed, with the real detector (or the regex layer that cuts it; the SM chooses and says why): the detector receives composed text and the written file holds no part of the name, as for the composed text;
   - **path 2, [A]:** a name typed composed in a decomposed document, and typed decomposed in a composed one: no « not found », every place written with the added name's pseudonym, one row; in a document holding both forms, both forms are replaced;
   - **path 3, [E]:** a correction typed composed on a decomposed name: no part of it in clear, one row;
   - **path 4, grouping:** the name written composed at one place and decomposed at another: one name in the review, one pseudonym in the file, one row; 10.13's count of names still to review counts it once;
   - **paths 5 and 6, desktop corrections, single document and batch:** a correction typed composed on a decomposed name, and in a document holding both forms: every place reached, no « Quentrel » in clear, one row; a name added by hand: the same result in both forms (a guard);
   - **path 7, reuse (`process` twice, `batch --workers 1`):** a name saved from a composed document and met decomposed in the next one, and the reverse: the same pseudonym, no second row; and with a database that already holds the decomposed row, written by today's code: the same pseudonym;
   - **path 8, `delete-mapping`:** a name saved decomposed, the name typed composed: listed, deleted, with its erasure entry; saved both ways: both listed and deleted; `list-entities --search` lists both;
   - **path 9:** tested by 10.17 and 10.19 when they rebase (Ordering); here, path 2's cases assert the same outcome on `main`'s [A];
   - **every reader:** `.txt`, `.md`, `.docx`, `.pdf` (a PDF whose text pdfplumber returns decomposed today), `.xlsx`, `.csv` and the structured `.xlsx` and `.csv` readers return composed text; a file written decomposed comes out composed (AC5);
   - **the end cut (Lionel, 2026-10-22, end cut "A: Refuse them too (Rec.)"):** with a row an earlier version saved cut at its end (an invented name, such as « Vorlan Zorbôme » saved as « Vorlan Zorbô »), a custom pseudonym equal to what the cut name could have been is refused, in the CLI and the desktop app, at set time and at finalization: nothing written, no row stored; finding, reusing and erasing stay as P0-1 decided (a document naming the full name gets a second pseudonym, and `delete-mapping` with the full name finds nothing);
   - **a mark with no composed letter (AC4):** « x » + U+0301 inside an invented name: one word for the 10.16 check and for 10.15's places of [A]; [A] reaches the whole word.

   Decomposed forms are made at run time with `unicodedata.normalize("NFD", …)`: no combining mark is written in a test file (10.16's rule). The strings go through `scripts/accuracy_heldout_blind_check.py`, as in 10.16. Existing tests stay green. An existing test changes only for a reason this story gives, stated in the story and, for each edited assertion, in the body of the commit that edits it: in 10.16's round-2 test files and the round-1 helpers they call, for AC1 (their stand-in detector now meets composed text; the desktop batch helper's wait), AC2 (a custom pseudonym typed decomposed is saved composed) or AC5 (decomposed bytes in a written file or a stored row); in any other test, for AC5 only.
7. **AC7 — G1, one accuracy run (Lionel's option A: "one accuracy run must show the scores unchanged"):** one regular `accuracy.yaml` run on the branch, at the PR's final code, shows the 16 lines identical to `38212303870`, cited by ID, the lines pasted in the PR. The run is required whatever files the diff touches. Any difference is explained in the story and the PR and approved by Lionel before the merge; a recall drop is G3 (stop and ask Lionel).
8. **AC8 — CHANGELOG:** `[Unreleased]` gets a "Fixed" entry: an accented name stored with its accent as a separate mark (as some PDFs and programs write it) was treated as a different name from the same name written the usual way: cut by the detector (« Mme Zo »), with the letters « Me » of a name such as « Mélora » left in clear before its pseudonym, « not found » with [A], left partly in clear by a correction, shown as two names in the review, given a second pseudonym in a later file, and not found or only partly erased by `delete-mapping`. Every file read, every typed name and every comparison with saved names now use the usual (composed) form, so a document storing its accents apart is now detected exactly as the same document written the usual way. The entry does not say that missed names are now found: on the PO's texts the places found go from 11 to 17 of 19, and one text loses two places found today, which its usual form misses too. It also says what changes: written files come out in that form, with the same look and different bytes, even where no name was replaced. It says that names an earlier version saved with a separate mark, or saved wrongly because of it, are still found and erased, except a name that lost its last letters, and that nothing in the database is rewritten. It also says that a custom pseudonym equal to a name an earlier version saved wrongly, a name cut at its end included, is refused. It says "already in v2.2.0" only for what was measured there (paths 1 to 4, 7 and 8; the SM measures the desktop paths 5 and 6 on v2.2.0 before the entry names them), and that the « Mme Zo » cut was never released.
9. **AC9 — User copy (Lionel, 2026-10-21, Qc "A: Use it (Rec.)"):** the `delete-mapping` screen's line for several saved pseudonyms, « You are about to delete the following N entity mappings (one per type of this name): » (`cli/commands/delete_mapping.py:304-305`), is untrue when it lists a name's composed and decomposed saved pseudonyms of one type (AC3; measured by the SM). It becomes, byte for byte, N the count: « You are about to delete the following N saved pseudonyms (all of this name, whatever its type): ». It is Lionel's 10.19 line (10.19 Q2) without « or its capitals », which 10.19 puts back when it lands. The one-row line stays as it is. The question as asked, word for word (the full text he was shown is in the story, Dev Notes "Decisions Recorded"): "10.20 Qc: the delete screen's line when one name is saved both ways. Use the proposed line now, or keep today's until 10.19?" The options: "A: Use it (Rec.)" — "« You are about to delete the following 2 saved pseudonyms (all of this name, whatever its type): ». Your 10.19 wording minus « or its capitals », which 10.19 adds back." / "B: Keep it until 10.19" — "Today's line stays until 10.19 replaces it. Nothing is released in between, but the line is false on the main branch until then." Any other new text is user copy that Lionel approves, word for word, before the story is done (Lionel, 10.19 Q2 and Q5b: never "row" or "ligne").
10. **AC10 — Codex:** Lionel, 2026-10-18: "codex is NOT writing code, ever. Codex is a reviewer."; "codex is the reviewer for bmad projects, period, and don't substitute with forge without telling me first"; "forge cato and co are part of this. which is good, pai should keep and using it BUT NOT WITH BMAD". The BMAD dev agent (Claude) writes the code and the tests, tests first; no Forge, Anvil or Cato on this story. Before the merge command, the PR's final head gets one read-only Codex review (`codex exec --sandbox read-only`, run by the orchestrator, one session); if the code changes after that review, the new head is reviewed again. If Codex cannot run, the work stops and Lionel is told; no review is reported that did not run.
11. **AC11 — Gates:**
    - **G1:** AC7. The PR states that the detector's code and patterns do not change, and pastes the run's lines.
    - **G3 and G5** are read from the AC7 run: its 16 lines hold the main-corpus and held-out lines, so identical lines mean no recall change and no held-out change. Any recall drop: stop and ask Lionel.
    - **G4:** never in the same PR as a detector change, or as another Epic 10 story.

12. **AC12 — Tests first, proven on CI (story-level).** Before any product code changes, the tests of Dev Notes "Test Plan" are committed, written by the BMAD dev agent (AC6, AC10). Every test that fails on the unfixed code is marked `xfail(strict=True, raises=AssertionError)`; its preconditions are `pytest.fail("PRE: …")` calls (never `assert`), so a broken precondition is a red failure even under that marker, and its expected failure is the first `assert` after them. The PR's CI on that commit is green only if each of them really fails, with an assertion, on every OS leg (the spaCy-marked one on the legs that run spaCy). The fix commit removes the markers and changes no assertion of the new tests.
13. **AC13 — What must not change (story-level).** A document, a typed name and a saved name written the usual (composed) way are read, compared, written and saved exactly as today; a document without accents is written byte for byte as today; compatibility forms (« ﬁ » and the like) are not changed; passphrases and file paths are not changed; the detector's code and patterns (`nlp/`, `resources/`) and `utils/french_patterns.py` do not change; no schema change, no flag, no new screen. Until Lionel answers Qa and Qb, no saved row is rewritten.
14. **AC14 — Consumer trace (story-level).** Dev Notes "Consumer Trace" lists every path of the four parts, today and after the fix: every reader and the paths that call it; every place a name is typed; every comparison with saved names; every word cut outside the detector. The dev re-checks every row at the fix commit and records any difference.
15. **AC15 — Isolation and invented strings (story-level; the standing rules).** No agent working on this story opens, lists, reads or greps anything under `tests/test_corpus/held_out/`, `tests/test_corpus/final_exam/` or `tests/accuracy/`. The new test files sit in `tests/unit/`, which both leakage guards scan, so every string in them is an invented name (Zórbalia, Quentrel, Zórbalia Quentrel, Vorlan Mestrix, Éloria Quentrel, Zórbaville, Zorbal Quentrix, Zox + U+0301 + rbalia, Xantor Vellion, Xantor Véllion, Xantor Quovix, Xantor Zoxrbalia, Xantorville, Zorba + « ﬁ » + x, Mélora Quentrel, Zorbôme Quentrel; v1.5: Zórbalia Mestrix, Quentrel Zórbalia, Vorlan Zórbalia, the passphrase Zórbalia_pass_A_123!), a main-corpus word pre-checked by the SM (Test Plan), the command's own confirmation word (approved by the PO), or a code identifier. See Dev Notes "Isolation Rules".

### AC precisions (story-level)

- **AC1, the readers:** "composed" is `unicodedata.normalize("NFC", text)`. The point is the return value of each reader: `read_file`'s plain-text branch (`utils/file_handler.py:84`), `read_pdf`'s extracted text (`:140`), `read_docx` (`:208`), `read_excel` (`:288`), `read_csv` (`:340`), and each cell's value in `read_excel_structured` (`utils/tabular_reader.py:126`) and `read_csv_structured` (`:219`). pdfplumber's `unicode_norm` is not enough (measured, Context "State"): `read_pdf` composes the text it returns. Every path reads through these: the CLI's `process` and `batch` (one file at a time and parallel) through `process_document` (text `core/document_processor.py:1252`; spreadsheets `_process_document_tabular` → `detect_entities_tabular`, `:1388`, `:1390`), the desktop single document through `DetectionWorker` → `detect_entities` (`:1132`), the desktop batch through `batch_worker.py:366` (with review) and `process_document` (without). The SM found no other reader of documents (Code Map). The written output files are read back by the desktop only to show them (`gui/workers/finalization_worker.py:139`, `processing_worker.py:186`): they are composed already. Sheet names are not changed (they are not pseudonymized today). Compatibility forms are not changed: NFC keeps « ﬁ » (guard G4).
- **AC2, the typed texts:** one point for the CLI review, `get_text_input` (`validation/ui.py:102`, `Prompt.ask` `:111`), which every typed text of the review goes through: [A]'s 'Entity text' (`validation/workflow.py:628`) and type (`:633`), [E] (`:334`), [C] (`:363`) and [T] (`:408`). The command arguments: the name of `delete-mapping` (`cli/commands/delete_mapping.py:42`), the `--search` of `list-entities` (`list_entities.py:45-47`, composed where it is compared, `search_entities`) and of `list-mappings` (`list_mappings.py:55-57`, compared at `:134`). The desktop: « Modifier le texte de l'entité » (`gui/screens/validation.py:714`), « Changer le pseudonyme » (`:743`, composed by `normalize_custom_pseudonym` through `mapping_key`), the database screen's search field (`gui/screens/database.py:167`, read at `:555`, compared at `:574` and in `gui/workers/database_worker.py:230`). The review's « Filtrer les entités... » field (found by the SM in v1.0, in AC2 since `8bd21c0`): `gui/widgets/entity_panel.py:116`, kept at `:441`, compared at `:223`; a decomposed text finds no name today (T25). The SM found no other place where a name is typed. **Not composed, on purpose:** passphrases (a database created with a decomposed passphrase must still open; composing them would lock such a database), file and folder paths, the `delete-mapping` confirmation word, `--reason`.
- **AC3, the comparisons with saved names:** every comparison goes through a key (`mapping_key`, `core/mapping_key.py:17`) or the repository. **Both sides composed:** `mapping_key` composes its text first, so every key the core, the desktop and the custom pseudonyms compute is composed, and every new row is saved composed (its full name, and its first and last names split from the key). `import-mappings` saves a new row with its names composed (until v1.14; since then it copies the names as the source saved them, QA's IMPORT-002: AC precisions, "QA's round 4"). The substring searches compare composed forms. **Rows already saved decomposed:** Open Point (a); the design of both options and their measured cost are in Dev Notes "Open Points (a) and (b), measured" and Fix Design, AC3. **Decided (Lionel, 2026-10-21, Qa "A: Find as they are (Rec.)"): option 1.** Every lookup also tries the ways an earlier version saved the name; no saved row is ever rewritten (the epic's reading and AC13; G6 pins it). **One person saved both ways (Lionel, Qb "A: Usual way's one (Rec.)"): rule A.** Later documents, written either way, use the pseudonym of the row saved the usual (composed) way; both rows stay saved (T29). **The erasure:** `delete-mapping` by name lists every row of the name in any form before the confirmation and deletes them in one transaction, each with its 10.12 ERASURE entry (T15, T16); `--id` then reports the other form as « 1 other row with the same name remains (type: PERSON). » (measured; true; 10.19's Q5b later says « mapping »). **The rows saved wrongly (AC3's second kind; the epic's Context "Two letters of a name in clear, and names saved wrongly"):** the repository reaches them by the keys an earlier version could have saved (`former_keys`, Fix Design, AC3): today's key steps on the decomposed name (« lora Quentrel » after a lone U+0301 for « Mélora Quentrel », « Je » + U+0301 + « ro » + U+0302 + « Quentrel » for a « …ôme » name), and, since v1.1 (the PO's M1), the key with « Maître » in front in its three case forms (« Maître », « maître », « MAÎTRE », each decomposed as an earlier version saw it), for every type (titles are stripped for every type), and with « à » or « À » in front for a LOCATION (prepositions are stripped for LOCATION only). Those are the only accented words of the title and preposition patterns (`utils/french_patterns.py:18`, `:27`). **The SM's choice, and why:** the repository adds these forms to the key it is handed, rather than the callers handing it the entity's text. (1) No method signature changes: v1.0 measured that a caller-side argument breaks an existing test whose stand-in repository takes two arguments (`test_document_processor_internals.py::test_interactive_separates_known_and_unknown`). (2) It reaches the old row from every mention of the name, with or without its title: a later document may write « Vorlan Mestrix » alone, whose text holds no « Maître », and `delete-mapping "Vorlan Mestrix"` has no text at all; both must reach the row « Maître Vorlan Mestrix » that today's composed reading keys as « Vorlan Mestrix » (one person). (3) It is the old rule applied again: today's code keys « Maître Vorlan Mestrix » and « Vorlan Mestrix » alike, so the old row is that person's row. **Collisions, measured:** over the app's 143,283 names (each first name with each last name, and each alone; the PO's check), 466,927 former keys, none equal to another name's key or decomposed key, none shared by two names; over the app's 220 places, 1,143 former keys, none. A composed comparison alone (a scan) misses every row saved wrongly, and option 2 cannot repair them.
- **AC3, the searches (v1.5; QA's SEARCH-001 on `87cd011`, the orchestrator's ruling (a), no new question):** Lionel answered Qa "A: Find as they are (Rec.)" on the option that reads "each time the app looks for a saved name, it also looks for the ways an earlier version could have saved it". A search by name is the app looking for a saved name. So the three full-name searches, `list-entities --search`, `list-mappings --search` and the desktop database search (its inline path and `DatabaseWorker`), also try the search text's former forms (`former_keys`), matched the way each search already matches: a substring of the saved name, composed and lowercased, with the forms of the row's own type (a LOCATION row with LOCATION's, any other row with PERSON's, as the lookups). A row an earlier version saved with letters lost is then listed by the person's full name, as AC3 says and the CHANGELOG entry ("still found") says: « lora Quentrel » after a lone U+0301 for « Mélora Quentrel », « Zorbô » + « Quentrel » (decomposed) for « Zorbôme Quentrel », and the same with a decomposed « Maître » in front (T31, T32). QA found such rows on 7 of its 24 old databases: on `87cd011`, `list-entities --search` and `list-mappings --search` with the full name missed all 7, and the desktop search missed the one QA tried, while reuse and `delete-mapping` reached all 24 and a part of the name (« Quentrel ») listed them. A former form that is only the search's own key (`mapping_key` of the search: its title or LOCATION preposition taken off) is not tried, so a search keeps its titles and prepositions as today (AC13): « Mme Éloria » lists no « Éloria Quentrel » (G13). The comparison with the pseudonym does not change. No epic change: the epic's AC3 already names the three searches among the comparisons that reach both kinds of old row.
- **AC3 and AC5, the Codex review of `186972b` (v1.7; the orchestrator's ruling `rgpd-analysis/r1020/codex/ruling.md`):** one saved-name rule, in `core/mapping_key.py`, for every lookup, the searches and the custom-pseudonym checks.
  - **What an earlier version saved, replayed (Codex P1-1, P1-4).** The old steps (titles taken off anywhere in the name, a LOCATION's leading preposition, spaces collapsed) are applied to each per-word mix of the name's composed and decomposed words, and to its two whole forms (every word decomposed, every word composed) with a decomposed « Maître » (each case form) at each word position, or a decomposed « à » or « À » in front of a LOCATION. **The bound:** per-word mixes for a name of up to 4 accented words (16 mixes); a longer name, its two whole forms only; a title or preposition, with the two whole forms only. Out of reach, as a stated limit: a row mixed inside one word, and a mixed row with a decomposed « Maître ». **The cost:** 500 lookups on 10,087 rows take 0.203 s against 0.144 s at `186972b`, about 0.1 ms more each, so a 50-name document waits under a hundredth of a second more; a name of 6 words with 4 accents takes 0.7 ms per lookup.
  - **Four kinds of old key.** "whole" (every letter kept); "titled" (every letter kept, with a decomposed title or preposition); "sign" (letters lost, leaving a word that starts with a lone accent mark, or fewer words than the text it came from: two words joined where a space was); "cut" (letters lost with no such sign: at the end of a name, or before a hyphen). A "cut" key can be the same text as another person's correct name (Codex P0-1), so lookups do not try it (Lionel, 2026-10-22, P0-1 "A: Only with a sign (Rec.)"; Decisions Recorded). The composed form of a "cut" key is never tried, and a search never uses one: otherwise a database with no old rows would list another name (AC13; T41).
  - **Rows imported by `import-mappings` (Codex P1-3).** Lookups also try the composed form of each "titled" and "sign" key; `import-mappings` keeps composing (G10; reversed in v1.14: it copies the names as saved, IMPORT-002). The SM chose this over "import stores today's key" because import cannot give back letters a row lost (« JérôQuentrel » would stay out of reach), because it would change what G10 pins, and because the lookups' composed forms reach rows composed by any path. v1.18: no longer tried (the third Codex review's P0-b: a composed old key can be another person's correct name; AC precisions, "The third Codex review").
  - **The custom-pseudonym checks (Codex P0-2, P1-2).** "Already stored" (Q1) holds when a row of the name's type holds any form a lookup of the name tries; the usual form's row is reported first (rule A). "A stored real name" (Q-C2) holds when a word run of the pseudonym, accents and capitals ignored, equals a stored name or that name as today's code keys it (a title or preposition an earlier version kept, taken off); or when one of the run's lookup forms equals a stored name, capitals ignored and accent marks kept (rows that lost letters). Keeping the marks matters: « Mézorvan Quovan » stays accepted when « Zorvan Quovan » is saved (G14).
  - **Reused pseudonyms (Codex P2; AC5).** The writers compose what they write, in text files and in every cell, so a pseudonym an earlier version saved decomposed is written composed.
  - **Databases with no old rows (AC13):** lookups, searches, deletions and the custom-pseudonym checks give today's results (G14).
- **AC3 and AC13, the second Codex review of `56feb3d` (v1.10; the orchestrator's ruling `rgpd-analysis/r1020/codex/ruling2.md`):** the forward replay of v1.7 stays for the exact keyed lookups only (reuse, erasure, Q1, `import-mappings`' duplicate check; 56 old databases reached). The two comparisons that scan the saved names, Q-C2 (set time and finalization) and the four searches, compare each saved name as saved (composed) **and** each text it may stand for, rebuilt backwards from the old title pattern. The repair only adds texts: the saved name itself is always compared (an all-caps « MAÎTRE » surname, or a whole name saved decomposed, would otherwise slip through).
  - **The old pattern, word for word (`utils/french_patterns.py`, the same file at v2.2.0, `43041e9` and `56feb3d`):** `FRENCH_TITLE_PATTERN = r"\b(?:Docteur|Professeur|Madame|Monsieur|Mademoiselle|Maître|Dr\.?|Pr\.?|Prof\.?|M\.?|Mme\.?|Mlle\.?|Me\.?)(?!\w)\s*"`, removed anywhere in the text, case ignored, again until nothing changes, then the text stripped (`strip_french_titles`); for a place, `FRENCH_PREPOSITION_PATTERN = r"^[\s]*(?:(?:d'|l')|(?:aux|au|des|du|de|à|en)\s+)"`, once (`strip_french_prepositions`). v2.2.0 saved that text (`_normalize_entity_text`); `43041e9` also collapsed spaces (`mapping_key`). On text whose accents are separate marks, a mark is not a word character: `\b` holds before a letter that follows a mark, and `(?!\w)` holds before a mark.
  - **The repair rules, each mapped to the pattern** (the title words are the 13 alternatives, 7 of them also with « . »: 20; « Maître » cannot match decomposed text):

    | Rule | In a saved name | What the old pattern did there | Texts added | The pattern's part |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | S1 | a word that starts with a lone mark (at the start, after a space, or after a non-letter such as « - ») | a title word started the word, its last letter carried the mark: `\b`, an alternative, `(?!\w)` before the mark | each title word put back before the mark (v1.18: one or more title words in a row, the last one carrying the mark) | `\b`, each alternative, `(?!\w)` |
    | S2 | a mark followed by a letter (any case: no capital needed) | a title word ended a word right after the mark, then the spaces: `\b` after the mark, an alternative, `(?!\w)`, `\s*` | each title word and a space put back after the mark (v1.18: one or more in a row, then a space) | `\b`, each alternative with `\.?`, `(?!\w)`, `\s*` |
    | S3 | a mark at the end of the name, or before a non-letter or another mark | the same with nothing, a non-letter or a mark after it: no sign is left | each title word put back after the mark (v1.18: one or more in a row, and a space before a non-letter other than a mark): **in the saved-real-name check only** (Lionel, 2026-10-22, end cut "A: Refuse them too (Rec.)"; never in a lookup or a search) | `(?!\w)` at the end or before a non-word character |
    | S4 | a word « Maître » with its circumflex (any case, composed or decomposed; v1.14, REPAIR-001: « Maitre » and « MAITRE » never matched the old pattern and are names) | the alternative `Maître` is composed, so a decomposed « Maître » was kept, anywhere titles were removed (anywhere); a composed one comes only from `import-mappings` | the name without that word; and, after an S2 site, without the « Maître » joined to the word before it | the alternative `Maître` |
    | S5 | a place whose first word is « à » (any case) | the preposition pattern's `à` is composed and anchored at `^`: a decomposed « à » was kept at the start only | the place without that word (never inside a name such as « Zorbal-à-Quentrix », an invented place) | `^`, `à` |

  - **Q-C2 (set time and finalization):** Lionel's 10.11 Q-C2 "Whole names only (Rec.)": a run of whole words of the pseudonym is refused when it equals, accents and capitals ignored (`fold`, as Q-C2 always compared), a saved name, that name as today's code keys it, or a whole text the saved name may stand for. One pattern per saved name that may stand for another text (it holds a mark, a « Maître » word or a place's leading « à »), any number of sites at once, compared with `fullmatch`; a quick piece test first.
  - **The searches:** the search as typed, composed and lowercased, as before (no title taken off: « Mme Éloria » lists no « Éloria Quentrel » (G13) and no « Éloria Maîtrequix » (T44), as at `87cd011`; the ruling's "then today's title strip" would list both, an AC13 change, Notes for the PO and PM, item 12). A saved name is listed when the search is a part of it as saved, composed and lowercased, or of a text it may stand for with the droppable words left out and one site repaired, unless that part lies wholly inside the title word put back (a search for « Madame » alone does not list every old name). **The bound:** one site at a time; a name with two repaired sites is listed by a part of it, not by its full name (the round trip: 4,500 of 18,600 two-site names).
  - **No gate on old rows (the ruling's item 3):** a row holds `first_seen_timestamp` and no version; the `schema_version` metadata row's `updated_at` is the time a 1.1.0 build created the file or converted a v2.2.0 file (10.7). On the 56 old databases (copies): the 24 rows that may stand for another text in the 25 files v2.2.0 made are older than that time, but the 24 in the 31 files later builds made (`43041e9`, prototypes) are newer. A gate would drop those, and it adds nothing: the repair is inert on a saved name with no mark, no « Maître » word and no leading « à » (every name today's code saves composed, but names holding a mark with no composed letter, which can only gain refusals of odd pseudonyms).
  - **Q-C2's parts after repair (the ruling's item 4):** a repaired text may have more words than the saved name (« JérôQuentrel » stands for « Jérôme Quentrel ») or fewer (S4, S5): runs of the pseudonym with that many words are compared with it, as whole names (Lionel's Q-C2). Q-I ("Not alone (Rec.)", linking words) applies to the pseudonym's runs and Q-G ("Keep the app's four whole") to the split of the pseudonym for Q-D: neither reads saved names, so neither changes. Q-D's saved parts are the rows' first and last names, which the repair does not touch (an old joined row keeps one part).
  - **The cost** (10,000 saved names): one custom-pseudonym check 0.17 s against 0.15 s at `56feb3d`, all decomposed 0.28 s the first time and 0.17 s after; one search 0.04 s against 0.02 s, all decomposed 0.51 s the first time and 0.04 s after.
- **AC3 and AC13, QA's round 4 (v1.14; QA's gate `79836af`, FAIL 70; the orchestrator's ruling `rgpd-analysis/r1020/orch/ruling_qa4.md`):**
  - **IMPORT-002: `import-mappings` copies the names as the source saved them.** The ruling's rule is "composes a row only when composing loses no sign". Composing a row that holds accent marks always removes the marks the reverse repair reads, and no rule can tell a row whose marks are a sign from a whole name saved decomposed: they are the same bytes. Measured: nfd(« ZorbôQuentrel ») is what the old version saved from nfd(« Zorbôme Quentrel »), nfd(« Zorbôde la Quentrel ») from nfd(« Zorbôme de la Quentrel »), nfd(« Jérôquentrel ») from nfd(« Jérôme quentrel »), and each is also the whole name saved decomposed. So the only rule that loses no sign is to compose nothing: the import copies `full_name`, `first_name` and `last_name` byte for byte. An imported row is then reached like the row it was in the source: the lookups (their forward forms hold the decomposed and mixed forms), the saved-real-name check and the four searches (the reverse repair reads its marks). **G10 (v1.5) reverses**: it pinned "composed" for a whole name saved decomposed; it now pins "copied as saved" (the same row, its marks kept) in the fix commit, for this reason. The epic's AC3 says "New rows are saved composed": an imported row is a copy of a saved row, not one the app reads from a document (Notes for the PO and PM, item 17). Nothing is rewritten and nothing new is stored (Lionel's Qa).
  - **No other path composes a stored name** (the SM's grep of `gdpr_pseudonymizer/` at `79836af`): the readers, the typed texts, the searches and the writers compose texts they read or write, never a saved row; new rows are saved from texts already composed (`mapping_key`, the readers: `core/document_processor.py:603`, `:733`, `:830`); 10.7's conversion copies the rows (`data/database.py:563`, `INSERT … SELECT`); `import-mappings` was the only one (`cli/commands/import_mappings.py:194-201`).
  - **REPAIR-001: S4 is « Maître » with its circumflex**, any case, composed or decomposed (`composed(word).casefold() == "maître"`): the only form of the word the old pattern could leave in a name. « Maitre » and « MAITRE » are names: a saved « Vorlan MAITRE » no longer stands for « Vorlan », so « Vorlan Quovix » is accepted and « Éloria Quentrel » lists no « Éloria MAITRE Quentrel », as at `56feb3d`.
- **AC3 and AC13, the third Codex review of `9784ddf` (v1.18; the orchestrator's ruling `rgpd-analysis/r1020/codex/ruling3.md`; no new question: P0-b applies Lionel's P0-1 answer, P0-a his end-cut answer):**
  - **P0-b: an old key is never tried in its composed form.** `saved_name_forms` (every lookup by name: reuse, erasure, Q1, `import-mappings`' duplicate check) gives the composed text, the keys an earlier version saved (`former_keys`: "whole", "titled", "sign") and the text as given; the composed form of a "titled" or "sign" key (v1.7, Codex P1-3) is no longer in the list. It was there for rows `import-mappings` composed; since v1.14 the import copies rows as saved, and no path composes a stored name (the SM's grep of `gdpr_pseudonymizer/` at `9784ddf`: every `composed(` and `normalize("NFC"` applies to a text read, typed, searched or written, never to a stored row), so no reader needs it (Consumer Trace rows 19, 20, 25 and 29). **The property:** every form other than the composed text and the text as given holds a sign, a separate accent mark: each text the replay tries but the all-composed one holds a decomposed accented letter, a decomposed « Maître » or, for a place, a decomposed « à », and no old step takes a mark off (the title pattern removes letters, a « . » and spaces; the preposition pattern letters, an apostrophe and spaces). So no form equals a name today's code saves (**corrected in v1.19:** not for a place whose key still starts with a preposition, « à de Zórbaville » keyed « de Zórbaville »: the old steps on the all-composed key gave « Zórbaville », no mark; v1.19 skips that text, T61), and a fresh composed « Vorlan ZorbôQuentrel » saved first is never reused, listed or erased through « Vorlan Zorbôme Quentrel » (CLI `process`, the desktop, `delete-mapping`: T51, T52; the property: T53). Measured over the sweep's texts (Reproduction Evidence, item 19): 0 of 30,443,050 extra forms without a mark on the prototype; 19,706,512 of 54,683,050 at `9784ddf`, all composed forms. A name holding a letter that has no composed form (no French letter: « r » with a circumflex) keeps its mark when today's code saves it, so it is outside "a composed text without a sign" (Notes for the PO and PM, item 21).
  - **P0-a, item 1: what the old step took off at one place, derived from `strip_french_titles`** (`utils/french_patterns.py:30-50`, the same file at v2.2.0, `43041e9` and `9784ddf`). It replaces every match of `FRENCH_TITLE_PATTERN` with nothing, case ignored (`re.sub(…, flags=re.IGNORECASE)`), strips the ends, and repeats until the text stops changing. A match is `\b`, one of the 20 title words, `(?!\w)`, then `\s*`. (a) Matches side by side form one stretch, taken off together: T1 S1 … Tk Sk, each Ti a title word and Si the spaces it ate. Inside a stretch each Si holds at least one space: with none, T(i+1)'s first letter follows Ti, and `(?!\w)` fails after Ti's last letter or, after Ti's « . », sees that letter (the engine then takes Ti without its « . », which stays and ends the stretch). (b) Sk is all the spaces there were (`\s*` is greedy), so a stretch is never followed by a space; Sk is empty when a non-word character follows (an accent mark, « - », « . », or the end) and holds at least one space before a word character. (c) A second pass takes nothing more: a title word the first pass left failed `\b` (a word character before it, which a pass never takes off, since a match that ends with a word character needs a non-word character after it) or `(?!\w)` (a word character after it, which no match starts with, since a match needs `\b` before it); the strip only takes spaces off the ends. Measured: over the sweep's texts, one pass always gave the loop's result (0 differences). (d) On text whose accents are separate marks, a mark is not a word character: `\b` holds after a mark and `(?!\w)` before one. Today's code composes first, so the two disagree only where a stretch has a mark beside it: after it (carried by Tk's last letter) or before it (the mark of the letter before T1). Folded (accents and capitals ignored, spaces collapsed), a stretch reads « t1 … tk », one space between two words. **So each site adds, optional, one or more title words in a row:** S1 (a mark at a word start or after a non-letter) the words before the mark, the last one carrying it; S2 (a mark before a word character) the words and one space; S3 (a mark before another mark, a non-letter or the end of the name; the saved-real-name check only) the words, and when a non-letter other than a mark follows, an optional space (the spaces eaten before « - » or « . »). As regexes over folded text, with `RUN = (?:TITLES)(?: (?:TITLES))*`: S1 `(?:RUN)?`, S2 `(?:RUN )?`, S3 `(?:RUN)?` or `(?:RUN ?)?`; the « Maître » joined after an S2 site `(?:RUN(?: maitre)?|maitre)`. Codex's row: the S3 site between U+0302 and U+0301 takes « me me », so « zorbome melora quentrel » is reached.
  - **What the saved-real-name check sees of it:** a custom pseudonym is a mapping key before any check (`normalize_custom_pseudonym`, `core/custom_pseudonym.py:107-116`), so a run of it holds no title word today's step takes off. Of a stretch, today's step keeps only the first word when a mark is before it (glued to the accented letter: no `\b` once composed) and the last word when a mark follows it (its accented letter is no title once composed); the words between have spaces on both sides and are taken off today too. So only the S3 site between two marks needs two words (« Zorbôme Mélora »); at the other sites one word reaches every pseudonym, and more words change no refusal (the mutants with one word at S1, at S2 or before the joined « Maître » survive every test and the sweep: equivalent mutants, Test Plan, v1.18). They are there because the ruling asks each site to allow what the old step could take off, and the derivation says it could.
  - **P0-a, item 2: Q-C2 refuses when either comparison matches; both only add refusals.** A run is refused when, folded, it equals a saved name or that name as today's code keys it (as before); when the reverse repair of a saved name reaches it (as before, with item 1); or when one of the run's lookup forms (`saved_name_forms(run, t)`, t a person's and a place's, after P0-b) equals a saved name that may stand for another text, capitals ignored and accent marks kept (v1.7's forward comparison, back beside the reverse repair). Only a name holding a sign can equal a form other than the run itself, which the folded comparison already covers. Set time and finalization call the same function. Keeping the marks matters as in v1.7 (G14 `custom_mezorvan`; G21). On the prototype the forward forms refuse no text the reverse repair does not (the sweep: 0 of 911,651; QA's 500,910 list names: 0); with a gap in the reverse repair (the mutant with one word at the S3 site) they still refuse the name as written (6,176 texts), not its spelling without accents (Notes for the PO and PM, item 18). T57 pins them with the reverse repair switched off.
  - **P0-a, item 3:** the spellings with other accents stay refused: « Zorbome Melora Quentrel » and « ZORBOME MELORA QUENTREL » as well as « Zorbôme Mélora Quentrel » (T54, T55, T56); the reverse repair compares folded text.
  - **The searches keep the reverse repair only** (the ruling's item 2), as v1.10 wrote them: the search as typed, composed and lowercased; one site at a time, S1 or S2, one title word put back. A name whose site lost two title words, or whose only site is S3 (Codex's row: the place between two marks), is listed by a part of it (« Quentrel », « lora Quentrel »), not by its full name: v1.10's bound, and Lionel's end-cut answer for S3 ("never in a lookup or a search"). The sweep: of the 380,926 texts the story's rules list by the full name, 0 missed; 527,408 in the bound.
  - **The class sweep (the ruling's proof; Reproduction Evidence, item 19):** every text of one to three words over this alphabet, each an invented name: the 20 title words in every case form (58 forms, « Maître » included), with and without « . »; the invented pieces « Zorb » and « lora »; the accented letters « ô » and « é » before a title word (« Zorbô » + a title word, « ô » + a title word) and after it (« é » + « lora »); a title word's last letter carrying U+0301 or U+0302 where that makes a composed letter (« Mé », « Mê », « Dŕ », « Ḿ »); one or two spaces between words; for places, « à » or « À » first; and, beyond the ruling's alphabet, « - » and « ' » with and without spaces between two words. One word, every combination (1,277 texts); two words, every word over every title form beside every word over five of them (« Me », « me. », « M », « Dr », « Madame »), both orders (741,100); three words over « Me », « m. » and « Dr » (48,560); places (4,788); « - » and « ' » (115,926). For each text X: Y, what the old steps saved from nfd(X); K, today's key of X (what X typed as a custom pseudonym becomes). **Q-C2:** X, and X without its accents, refused with Y saved whenever refused with K saved: 0 misses on the prototype; 12,246 and 11,370 at `9784ddf`. **The repair reaches fold(K):** all 911,651 on the prototype; 15,250 missed at `9784ddf`. **fold(X) itself** (the ruling's words): reached for 807,561; the other 104,090 all hold a title word today's step also takes off where no mark is (« Madame » before a plain word: no site there, and Q-C2 never sees that word, `normalize_custom_pseudonym` takes it off); none is missed for another reason (23,474 at `9784ddf`). **Searches:** 0 missed. **The forward property:** 0 extra forms without a mark. **Declared limits:** none in this alphabet; the only one is the searches' bound, as in v1.10. **Corrected in v1.19:** the alphabet missed a class (« Maître » joined by « - », « ' » or « . », or glued after a lost-letter place) that the PO's sample found; v1.19 adds rules for it; the PO's NO-GO of v1.19 found one more family, fixed in v1.21 (AC precisions, "The PO's NO-GO of v1.18 and of v1.19").
  - **False refusals and false search matches, the round-4 sets again: 0 new.** QA's 500,910 list names against the 29 distinct rows that may stand for another text in 86 old databases (QA's 84 and the SM's 82, copies; 90 rows): 1 refused (« Jérôme », the original of an end-cut row), as at `9784ddf`; the forward forms refuse none; two list words list a row only through a repair (« Jérôme », true; « Léo », SEARCH-003, backlog), as at `9784ddf`. The SM's round-4 probe on its 82 databases: Q-C2 finds the original of 48 of the 48 rows whose original is known, the full-name search 43, false repairs over the 143,283 names of `french_names.json` 0, as at `9784ddf`. Clean databases (`probes/clean117.py`): 10,000 names of the app's list saved composed, none of them a name that may stand for another text; 1,000 list names checked as custom pseudonyms (500 refused, the saved ones) and 200 list words searched (6,720 rows listed): the same results, byte for byte, on `9784ddf` and on the prototype (one digest each). In the sweep, a name spelled without its accents is refused through its old row where a fresh row would not refuse it in 8,800 texts (8,608 at `9784ddf`): the same person's name, never another's; the 192 more are names whose « à » reads « a » or whose last title word becomes a title once the accents are gone (« ZorbôDr 'Dŕ » typed « ZorboDr 'Dr », kept as « ZorboDr ' »).
  - **The cost** (10,000 saved names, the minimum of 5 rounds): one custom-pseudonym check 0.147 s against 0.145 s at `9784ddf`, all decomposed 0.162 s against 0.158 s (the first one 0.30 s against 0.33 s); one search 0.008 s against 0.008 s, all decomposed 0.033 s against 0.032 s (the first one 0.20 s against 0.24 s). Round 4's prototype, with the same probe: 0.17 s and 0.04 s. The prototype adds at most a few thousandths of a second to `9784ddf`.
- **AC3 and AC13, the PO's NO-GO of v1.18 and of v1.19 (v1.19, v1.21; the orchestrator's rulings of 2026-10-22: fix in this story, no question; the PO's findings in `rgpd-analysis/r1020/po6/` and `po7/`):**
  - **What the check compares.** Y, what an earlier version saved (the title steps and, for a place, the preposition step, on the text as read, decomposed or mixed), against K, today's key (the same steps on the composed text); a custom pseudonym has K's shape (`normalize_custom_pseudonym` is `mapping_key`). They differ where a step matches differently on the two forms: next to an accent mark (`\b`, `(?!\w)`), and at « Maître » and « à », the only words of the two patterns with an accent.
  - **The table** (S1 to S5: the repair's rules, `core/legacy_names.py`):

    | Row | What differs | Example (Y: the old row; K: today's key) | Rule |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | a1 | `\b` before a title right after a mark | « Zorbôme Quentrel »: Y nfd(« Zorbô ») + « Quentrel » | S2; S3 (Q-C2 only, Lionel's end-cut answer) |
    | a2 | `(?!\w)` after a title whose last letter carries a mark | « Mélora Quentrel »: Y U+0301 + « lora Quentrel » | S1 |
    | a3 | title words between two marks | « Zorbôme Mélora Quentrel » | S3, one or more title words (v1.18) |
    | a4 | the spaces the matches ate | « ZorbôDr DR.lora »: K « ZorbôDr .lora » | S2's space; S3's optional space (v1.18) |
    | b1 | « Maître » as a whole word | « Maître Éloria Quentrel » | S4 (v1.10) |
    | b2 | « Maître » joined by « - », « ' » or « . » | « Quovôme-Maître Zorbal »: K « Quovôme-Zorbal » | S4 joined (v1.19) |
    | b3 | « Maître » glued after a mark, title words taken off between | « Zorbôme Maître-Zorbal »: K « Zorbôme -Zorbal » | S4 after an S2 site, only with title words put back (v1.19) |
    | b5 | « à » first in a place | « à Zórbaville » | S5 (v1.10) |
    | b6 | a place's preposition after a leading « Maître » (the old step took none) | « Maître de Zorbôme », « Maître d'Zorbôme »; « Maître d'Maître Zorbôme Quentrel » (v1.21) | the first kept word, or its « d' » or « l' », left out (v1.19); with it a « Maître » glued to the apostrophe (v1.21) |
    | b7 | a place made only of « Maître » words after a preposition | « de Maître »: K « de » | a preposition alone (v1.19) |
    | c1 | the title loop | — | nothing: one pass gives the result |
    | c2 | spaces kept (v2.2.0) or collapsed | — | nothing: `fold` collapses both sides |
    | c4 | a word stored composed in an old row (a mixed row) | a composed « Maître » or « à » | nothing: the old step treated it as today does |
    | c6 | today's place key is not idempotent (« à de Zórbaville ») | — | the forward replay skips the all-composed key (v1.19, T61) |

    **Removed by Lionel (2026-10-22):** a5 (the Tamil, Myanmar and Balinese marks) and b4 (the Turkish « İ » in « Monsieur » and « Mademoiselle »), with their code, test cases and mutants. **Out of scope:** c3 (unusual space characters) and c5 (letters with no composed form), which never had rules. His words: "the exotic ones are useless, and frankly they are just bloat." and "you're overcorrecting. i told you to remove the ones you called yourself "exotic". keep the french looking but artificial, and the plausible"
  - **The searches** keep v1.10's rules (whole droppable words; one S1 or S2 site; one title word). A name whose saved form holds « Maître » inside a word, or whose key is title words only, is listed by a part: v1.10's bound.

On the v1.21 prototype, 0 misses in: the PO's place sample (`po7_sample_place.py`, seeds 11, 22, 33 and 44; 120,000 texts), its French sample (`po7_sample_fr.py`, seeds 7171, 8282 and 9393; 90,000 texts), its round-6 sample (`po6_sample.py`, seeds 1022 and 2026; 40,000 texts), and the SM's sweep (2,204,243 texts of up to three words: titles in every case, accented letters, « Maître » joined or glued, places with « à » and a preposition). The v1.19 prototype had 37 misses in the PO's place sample. These are samples, not a proof.

- **AC4, the word cuts outside the detector, all of them (Code Map, "Word cuts"):**
  - `core/custom_pseudonym.py`: `_WORD` (`:267`), used by `document_words` (`:298`) and `_capitalised_occurrence` (`:319`). A word is a run of word characters and combining marks (T20).
  - `validation/models.py`: `_NOT_AFTER_WORD` and `_NOT_BEFORE_WORD` (`:32-33`), the boundaries of `places_of` (`:292`). A combining mark is part of the word on either side: « Zox » is no place inside « Zox » + U+0301 + « rbalia » (T21), and the whole word typed is one place (G2). `is_listed_place` (`:314`) compares texts and spans and cuts nothing.
  - `validation/workflow.py`: `_locate_modified_span` (`:592`, `str.find` `:613`, `:617`) cuts no words, but can stop between a letter and its marks: a match that starts on a mark or ends just before one is skipped.
  - `core/document_processor.py:1079`, `_sanitize_error_message`'s name-shaped run (found by the SM in v1.0, in AC4 since `8bd21c0`), which keeps names out of the log and the audit entry: a name holding a mark with no composed letter is not redacted today (T22). Its small-letter class takes the marks, and its end-of-word rule is kept.
  - **Found by the SM, not changed here:** `FRENCH_TITLE_PATTERN` (`utils/french_patterns.py:18`) ends a title where `(?!\w)` holds; it is used by `mapping_key` (`:32`), the replacement prefix (`core/document_processor.py:651-682`, through `assignment_engine.strip_titles`), the previews (`:1172`; `validation/workflow.py:538`, `:565`) and by the detector. It is shared with the detector and is one of the 13 files the accuracy suite loads, so it is the detector's (G4; Open Point (c)). After AC1 every French title followed by an accent sees a composed letter (every pair of a French letter and its accent has one); a mark with no composed letter right after a title word stays a follow-up, with Open Point (c). `nlp/entity_grouping.py` (`_is_person_variant`, the review's grouping) is in `nlp/` and receives composed text.
  - "Every character for which `unicodedata.combining` is not 0" is 912 characters at the Unicode version of Python 3.11 (14.0.0). 1,950 characters are non-spacing marks, 1,063 of them with combining class 0 (mostly vowel signs of scripts written in India and South-East Asia); AC4's rule leaves those as today. Every accent of French has a class above 0. The SM keeps AC4's rule as written (Notes for the PO and PM, item 6).
- **AC5, the written files:** the text file is written from the composed text (`write_file`, `:343`; desktop `finalize_document`, `:1220`); each cell of `.xlsx` and `.csv` is written from the composed cell (`write_excel`, `utils/tabular_writer.py:17`; `write_csv`, `:76`; `write_tabular_as_text`, `:126`). So a document stored decomposed comes out composed even where no name was replaced (T2); a document without accents is unchanged byte for byte (G3). `.docx` and `.pdf` are written as `.txt`, as today.

## Tasks / Subtasks

> **Order is mandatory.** Task 0 checks the starting state, Lionel's answers and the merge order (10.16 first). Task 1 writes the tests and proves every failure on the unfixed code, on every CI leg (AC12). Task 2 implements the fix. Task 3 removes the markers, applies the existing-test edits that 10.16's round-2 files need, and runs G6 locally. Task 4 re-checks the consumer trace. Task 5 is the CHANGELOG. Task 6 is the accuracy run (AC7), CI, the PR marked Ready for Review, and the orchestrator's read-only Codex review of the PR's final head. Task 7 is QA, before any merge. The dev never merges. **The dev agent is the BMAD dev agent (Claude)**: it writes the tests first, then the fix (AC6, AC10). No other agent writes code on this branch, and no `codex` session that writes runs on it: Codex only reviews, read-only, before the merge command (Task 6.3). If Codex cannot run, stop and tell Lionel; no review is reported that did not run.
>
> **v1.5 (QA's gate `3afe46e`, the orchestrator's ruling):** development reopens on the same branch and PR, in this order: 1.8 (the new tests first, with CI), 2.8 (the fix), 3.5 (markers, local G6, CI), 4.2, then Task 6 again on the new head (6.1 to 6.7; the read-only Codex review on the final head), then QA's re-check (7.7). The same rules: the BMAD dev agent (Claude) writes the tests and the code; Codex only reviews, read-only, before the merge command.
>
> **v1.7 (the Codex review of `186972b`, the orchestrator's ruling `rgpd-analysis/r1020/codex/ruling.md`):** first the PO's check and Lionel's P0-1 answer; then, on the same branch and PR: 1.9 (the new tests first, with CI), 2.9 (the fix), 3.6 (markers, the AC5 test edits, local G6, CI), 4.3, Task 6 again on the new head (6.1 to 6.7: the G1 run and a second read-only Codex review on the final head), then QA's re-check (7.8). The same rules: the BMAD dev agent (Claude) writes the tests and the code; Codex only reviews, read-only.
>
> **v1.10 (the second Codex review of `56feb3d`, the orchestrator's ruling `rgpd-analysis/r1020/codex/ruling2.md`):** first the PO's check and Lionel's end-cut answer; then, on the same branch and PR: 1.10 (the new tests first, with CI), 2.10 (the fix), 3.7, 4.4, Task 6 again on the new head (the G1 run; a third read-only Codex review on the final head), then QA's re-check (7.9). The same rules.
