- **The ruling on the Codex review of 10.16 (Decisions Recorded, item 2), word for word from `rgpd-analysis/r1016/codex/ruling.md`, its "Finding 1 (Codex P0)" section:**

- Codex: for a reviewed PERSON written with a decomposed accent, `quentrel zórbalia` (o + U+0301), the custom pseudonym `Xantor Zórbalia` is accepted at set time and at finalization. The words are cut before accents are folded, so the real surname splits into `zo` and `rbalia` (a combining mark is not a word character). The written file then holds « Xantor Zórbalia a signé le contrat. » and the row is stored: the real surname is readable, through the CLI and the desktop.
- Checked by the orchestrator: no code in the product normalizes Unicode at document load (`unicodedata` is used only for folding, custom_pseudonym.py:205-206 and hybrid_detector.py:298). So every comparison in 10.16's checks sees decomposed text as is.
- **Ruling: fix it in 10.16, inside the checks, without changing what is written.** Every comparison the #8, #6 and #7 rules make (the document's words, the review's names, the pseudonym's runs, the guard's values, the reach's names) reads its texts in one composed form before cutting them into words (Unicode NFC, then the existing folding), so a word with a combining mark stays one word. The written file and the stored rows keep the document's own characters: no normalization of the document or the output in this story.
- Tests (tests first, strict xfail on 463481a, assertions on written files and stored rows, CLI process and batch --workers 1, desktop single document and batch): the reviewed name written decomposed and the custom pseudonym written composed; the reverse (name composed, pseudonym decomposed); both decomposed; at set time and at finalization (a name added after the pseudonym). Each: refused with Lionel's Q1 reason (or the existing reason at set time), nothing written, no row. Guards: the same cases with no shared surname are accepted as today. Invented names only, through the blind word check.

- **10.16's follow-up (f), which belongs here (AC4), word for word from the 10.16 story on its branch (`story/10.16-custom-pseudonym-fixes`, round 2), "Follow-ups Found by the SM":**

- **(f) (round 2):** NFC leaves a mark that has no composed letter outside its word (AC precisions, AC1, round 2): "Xantor Zoxrbalia" next to « quentrel zox » + U+0301 + « rbalia » is still accepted on the prototype. No French letter is concerned. A cut that keeps every combining mark inside its word (for example, a word pattern that keeps inside the word every character for which `unicodedata.combining` is not 0) would close it; not in the ruling, so backlog unless the orchestrator routes it.

- **Lionel's answers to this story's three questions (2026-10-21)**, word for word from the decision log, "Story 10.20 Qa", "Story 10.20 Qb" and "Story 10.20 Qc", each with the full question he was shown, word for word from the orchestrator's `questions-10.20-for-lionel.md` (PO-vetted v1.1, Qa with the idle numbers):

  **Qa, the decision-log entry:**

- Question: the full Qa text of questions-10.20-for-lionel.md was shown in the reply, then asked: "10.20 Qa: names already saved the other way. Find them as they are, or rewrite them once?" — Options: "A: Find as they are (Rec.)" (Nothing in your database changes. Every lookup also tries the ways an older version saved the name, wrongly saved ones included. About a hundredth of a second more per 50-name document.) / "B: Rewrite them once" (The first time 2.3.0 opens your database, names saved the other way are rewritten (0.3 s for 10,000). Wrongly saved names and people saved twice still need A's lookups, so B adds a database change on top of A.) — **Lionel: "A: Find as they are (Rec.)"**

  **Qa, the full question shown:**

"10.20 Qa: some documents store an accented letter as the plain letter followed by a separate accent mark; on screen it looks the same. Until now the app saved names read from such documents that way, so a later document writing the name the usual way did not find them: the person got a second pseudonym, and « delete-mapping » with the name said « Entity Not Found » (measured, v2.2.0 and today). From 2.3.0 every document and every name you type is read the usual way. For names already saved the other way, two choices.

(A) Find them as they are, change nothing in your database (Rec.): each time the app looks for a saved name, it also looks for the ways an earlier version could have saved it. That also finds names an earlier version saved wrongly: « Mélora Quentrel » was saved as « lora Quentrel » (5 of the 111 accented names of the app's own name list lose letters this way), and a name written after « Maître » was saved with « Maître » in front. Measured on 10,000 invented names: looking up 500 names takes 0.15 s instead of 0.11 s; a document of 50 names waits about a hundredth of a second longer.

(B) Rewrite them once: the first time 2.3.0 opens your database, each name saved the other way is rewritten the usual way (0.3 s for 10,000 names; the log says how many, never a name). The names saved wrongly cannot be repaired that way, and a person saved both ways cannot be rewritten without dropping one of their two pseudonyms (the next question), so the app still needs (A)'s lookups: (B) only adds a one-time change to your database.

Which one?"
Options: "A: Find them as they are (Rec.)" / "B: Rewrite them once".

  **Qb, the decision-log entry:**

- Question: the full Qb text of questions-10.20-for-lionel.md was shown in the reply, then asked: "10.20 Qb: one person saved twice, once each way. Which pseudonym do later documents use?" — Options: "A: Usual way's one (Rec.)" (Documents written the usual way, nearly all of them, keep exactly the pseudonym they get today. Only documents storing the accent apart change (« Anya Branco » for both).) / "B: The oldest one" (The person keeps the first pseudonym they got. When the other way came first, usual documents change pseudonym (today's « Nassim Fonseca » would become « Noé Fonseca »).) — **Lionel: "A: Usual way's one (Rec.)"**

  **Qb, the full question shown:**

"10.20 Qb: a database written by v2.2.0 or by today's version can hold the same person twice, saved once each way, with two pseudonyms. Measured on v2.2.0: a document writing « Zórbalia Quentrel » the usual way, then one storing its accent apart, gave « Anya Branco », then « Lise Branco ». From 2.3.0 both count as one name, so later documents use one of the two.

(A) The pseudonym saved the usual way (Rec.): documents written the usual way keep the pseudonym they get today; only documents storing the accent apart change (measured: « Anya Branco » for both).

(B) The oldest pseudonym: the person keeps the first one they got; when the other way came first, documents written the usual way change pseudonym (measured: a person who gets « Nassim Fonseca » today would get « Noé Fonseca »).

Either way both pseudonyms stay saved, « delete-mapping » with the name lists and deletes both, each with its own erasure entry, and nothing is left in clear (measured). Which one?"
Options: "A: The usual way's pseudonym (Rec.)" / "B: The oldest pseudonym".

  **Qc, the decision-log entry:**

- Question: the full Qc text of questions-10.20-for-lionel.md was shown in the reply, then asked: "10.20 Qc: the delete screen's line when one name is saved both ways. Use the proposed line now, or keep today's until 10.19?" — Options: "A: Use it (Rec.)" (« You are about to delete the following 2 saved pseudonyms (all of this name, whatever its type): ». Your 10.19 wording minus « or its capitals », which 10.19 adds back.) / "B: Keep it until 10.19" (Today's line stays until 10.19 replaces it. Nothing is released in between, but the line is false on the main branch until then.) — **Lionel: "A: Use it (Rec.)"**

Proposed (the number is the count): « You are about to delete the following 2 saved pseudonyms (all of this name, whatever its type): », the line you approved for 10.19 without « or its capitals »; 10.19 puts those words back when it lands. (A) Use it (Rec.). (B) Keep today's line until 10.19 replaces it: nothing is released in between, but the line stays false until then. Which one?"
Options: "A: Use it (Rec.)" / "B: Keep it until 10.19".

  **P0-1, the decision-log entry** (2026-10-22, after the Codex review of `186972b`, word for word from the decision log, "Story 10.20 P0-1"):

- Full text shown and question asked: questions-10.20-for-lionel.md, section "P0-1". Includes the correction of Qa's « 5 of the 111 accented names » to « 5 of 106 » (five names counted twice).
- **Lionel: "A: Only with a sign (Rec.)"** → a lost-letter former key is used only when the loss left a sign (a word starting with a lone mark; two words glued); end-of-name cuts are not recovered by the full name (second pseudonym as in 2.2.0); the reverse case (an old cut row reached by the real shorter name) stays, as no option closes it without losing composed/decomposed whole-name rows.

  **P0-1, the full question shown** (the PO's rewrite, word for word from `questions-10.20-for-lionel.md`, "P0-1"):

Shown in the reply, word for word (preceded by: « One correction first: the Qa question you answered yesterday said « 5 of the 111 accented names ». It's 5 of **106**: five names are in both lists and were counted twice. The reviewer caught it. »):

"10.20 P0-1: names an earlier version saved with their last letters missing.

Your Qa answer was "A: Find as they are (Rec.)", on the text « each time the app looks for a saved name, it also looks for the ways an earlier version could have saved it. That also finds names an earlier version saved wrongly ». The final review found a case where this finds the wrong person.

When a document stored its accents apart, versions up to 2.2.0 sometimes dropped letters from the saved name. Usually what is left looks like no real name: « Mélanie » was saved as « lanie » with a stray accent in front, and « Jérôme Quentrel » as « JérôQuentrel », the space gone. Those are found safely.

But when the dropped letters are the last ones, what is left can be another real name: « Jérôme » written alone was saved as « Jérô ». With made-up names: « Vorlan Zorbôme » was saved exactly as a person really called « Vorlan Zorbô » is saved, so the app cannot tell the two apart. Measured on the 2.3.0 code as it stands (not released): with « Vorlan Zorbô » saved, a document naming « Vorlan Zorbôme » gets « Vorlan Zorbô »'s pseudonym, and « delete-mapping "Vorlan Zorbôme" » erases « Vorlan Zorbô »'s saved pseudonym, which cannot be undone. It also happens the other way round: with « Vorlan Zorbôme » saved by an earlier version, a document naming « Vorlan Zorbô » gets « Vorlan Zorbôme »'s pseudonym, and « delete-mapping "Vorlan Zorbô" » erases it.

In the app's own name lists, 5 of its 106 accented names lose letters. Four always leave a sign (« Médéric », « Mélanie », « Mélissa », « Ménard »); « Jérôme » leaves one before a surname, but not when written alone or as a surname. Across 500,962 combinations of the app's two name lists, no name that lost letters is the same as another person's name. Real names are not limited to these lists.

(A) Only when the loss left a sign (Rec.): names that lost letters with a sign are still found. A name that lost its last letters (« Jérô ») is no longer found by its full name: that person gets a second pseudonym in later documents, as in 2.2.0, and « delete-mapping » with the full name finds nothing; typing what was saved (« Jérô ») still erases it, and a search for part of the name (« Jér ») lists it. This removes one of the two wrong cases: « Vorlan Zorbôme » never gets « Vorlan Zorbô »'s pseudonym. The other stays, because the saved name is the same either way: if « Vorlan Zorbôme » was saved by an earlier version, a later « Vorlan Zorbô » still gets that pseudonym, and erasing « Vorlan Zorbô » erases it. Removing that too would mean no longer finding names like « André » or « Chloé » saved by an earlier version, which are far more common.

(B) All of them, as now: every name that lost letters is still found by its full name, the last-letter ones too, and both wrong cases stay."

Then asked: "10.20 P0-1: names an earlier version saved with their last letters missing. Find them only when the loss left a sign, or all of them?" — Options: "A: Only with a sign (Rec.)" (Removes one of the two wrong-person cases. A name cut at its very end (« Jérô ») gets a second pseudonym, as in 2.2.0. The other case can't be closed without losing far more common names like « André ».) / "B: All of them" (Every cut name is still found by its full name, the last-letter ones too, and both wrong-person cases stay.)

Shown in the reply, word for word:
"10.20 end cut: should the pseudonym check also refuse what a cut name could have been?

Your P0-1 answer was "A: Only with a sign (Rec.)", on the text « A name that lost its last letters (« Jérô ») is no longer found by its full name ». That answer is about finding, reusing and erasing saved names, and it stays as you decided.

The second final review found the same kind of gap in another check. When you choose a pseudonym yourself, the app refuses it if it is a real name already saved. Two gaps of that check with names an earlier version saved wrongly are now fixed without a question: with « Mélora Quentrel » saved as « lora Quentrel », you can no longer choose « Mélora Quentrel » or « Melora Quentrel ». One gap is left, and it is P0-1's end cut: with « Jérô » saved by an earlier version (from « Jérôme »), the check does not see « Jérôme », so you can choose « Jérôme » as someone else's pseudonym, and that real name is then written in the document. The same happens with a compound first name cut before its hyphen (made-up example: « Jérôme-Zorbal Quentrel » saved as « Jérô-Zorbal Quentrel »). Measured on the code with the two fixes.

This check only ever refuses a pseudonym you choose: it never gives anyone's pseudonym to someone else and never erases anything. So treating « Jérô » as possibly « Jérôme » here costs only some refusals.

Measured: of the app's own 728 names, 1 is cut this way when written alone or last (« Jérôme »); of its 1,149 pseudonym names, 2 (« Côme », « Jérôme »). The cost: when an earlier version saved a name with its accents apart and its last letter is accented (8 names of the app's list end that way, such as « Chloé »), 20 made-up names would also be refused as pseudonyms (« Chloéme », « Chloém », …); none of them is a name in the app's lists. In the 56 test databases (made with made-up names to cover these cases), the 5 saved names cut at their end would be protected, and the 3 saved names of a person really called « Vorlan Zorbô » would also refuse « Vorlan Zorbôme » as a pseudonym.

(A) Refuse them too (Rec.): a pseudonym equal to what a cut name could have been is refused, like any real name already saved. The wrong outcome is then the other way: a pseudonym that is no real name is sometimes refused (« Vorlan Zorbôme » when « Vorlan Zorbô » is saved), and you choose another one. Finding, reusing and erasing names stay as you decided in P0-1.

(B) Keep it as it is: the check does not look at cut names. The wrong outcome: « Jérôme » can be chosen as a pseudonym when « Jérô » was saved, and that real name is then written in the document.

Why A is recommended: a wrong refusal costs you one more choice; a wrong acceptance writes a real name into a document."
Then asked: "10.20 end cut: should the pseudonym check also refuse what a cut name could have been?" — Options: "A: Refuse them too (Rec.)" (« Jérôme » is refused as a pseudonym when « Jérô » was saved. Wrong outcome: a few made-up pseudonyms are refused too, and you choose another. Finding, reusing, erasing stay as in P0-1.) / "B: Keep it as it is" (The check ignores cut names. Wrong outcome: « Jérôme » can be chosen as a pseudonym when « Jérô » was saved, and that real name is written in the document.)

  Lionel's answer, as cut out of that entry: "A: Refuse them too (Rec.)".

  In short: **Qa "A: Find as they are (Rec.)"**: option 1; every lookup by name also tries the ways an earlier version saved it (the decomposed key, the key cut by the title pattern, the key with « Maître » or « à » in front); no saved row is ever rewritten (AC3, AC13; T12-T14, T27, T28, G6). **Qb "A: Usual way's one (Rec.)"**: rule A; when a name and type are saved both ways, later documents use the composed row's pseudonym; both rows stay saved, and `delete-mapping` with the name lists and deletes both, each with its entry (AC3; T16, T29). **Qc "A: Use it (Rec.)"**: `delete-mapping`'s several-row line is « You are about to delete the following N saved pseudonyms (all of this name, whatever its type): », N the count (AC9; T30).
  **P0-1 "A: Only with a sign (Rec.)":** a key an earlier version saved with letters lost is tried only when the loss left a sign (a word starting with a lone accent mark; two words joined where a space was); a name cut at its end is not reached by its full name and gets a second pseudonym, as in v2.2.0; the reverse case stays (Notes for the PO and PM, item 11; G15). → AC3 (the epic, `315eb24`), Fix Design item 1, T33.
  **End cut "A: Refuse them too (Rec.)":** the saved-real-name check (custom pseudonyms, set time and finalization) also compares what a name cut at its end could have been (S3), so « Jérôme » is refused as a pseudonym when « Jérô » was saved; finding, reusing, erasing and searching stay as P0-1 decided (no cut there). → AC3, AC6 and AC8 (the epic, `0a531a0`, `9f0ccd5`), Fix Design "The second Codex review", item 1, T46, T47.
- **The Codex rule (Lionel, 2026-10-18; epic AC10):** Codex is a reviewer and never writes code. The BMAD dev agent (Claude) writes the code and the tests, tests first; before the merge command the PR's final head gets one read-only `codex exec` review, run by the orchestrator, again only if the code changes after it; if Codex cannot run, the work stops and Lionel is told; no review is reported that did not run.

- [ ] Text after 10.20 (Lionel, 2026-10-20, option A): every file read, every typed name and every comparison with saved names use the composed form of accented letters (Unicode NFC). A document whose accents are stored as separate marks is written composed, the same look with different bytes, also where no name was replaced, and the CHANGELOG says so. No schema change, and no saved row is rewritten: every lookup by name also tries the ways an earlier version saved the name (Lionel, 2026-10-21, Qa); wrongly saved rows are reached only where the loss of letters left a sign, and a name that lost its last letters is not reached by its full name (Lionel, 2026-10-22, P0-1 "A: Only with a sign (Rec.)"), while the check that refuses a custom pseudonym equal to a saved real name also refuses what a name cut at its end could have been (Lionel, 2026-10-22, end cut "A: Refuse them too (Rec.)"); when a name and type are saved both ways, later documents use the pseudonym of the row saved the usual way, both rows staying saved (Qb) (Story 10.20, Open Points (a) and (b)). The `delete-mapping` screen's line for several saved pseudonyms becomes « You are about to delete the following N saved pseudonyms (all of this name, whatever its type): » (Qc, approved word for word; Story 10.20, AC9)

### Open Points (verbatim from the epic)

- **(a) Saved names written decomposed (AC3): decided (Lionel, 2026-10-21, Qa "A: Find as they are (Rec.)").** The question as asked, word for word (the full text he was shown, with the SM's and the PO's measurements, is in the story, Dev Notes "Decisions Recorded"): "10.20 Qa: names already saved the other way. Find them as they are, or rewrite them once?" The options: "A: Find as they are (Rec.)" — "Nothing in your database changes. Every lookup also tries the ways an older version saved the name, wrongly saved ones included. About a hundredth of a second more per 50-name document." / "B: Rewrite them once" — "The first time 2.3.0 opens your database, names saved the other way are rewritten (0.3 s for 10,000). Wrongly saved names and people saved twice still need A's lookups, so B adds a database change on top of A." The full text he was shown said « 5 of the 111 accented names »; the right count is 5 of 106 (five names are in both of the app's name lists and were counted twice), and Lionel was told so before P0-1 (2026-10-22). So every lookup by name also tries the ways an earlier version saved the name, the wrongly saved ones included where the loss of letters left a sign (Lionel's P0-1, AC3), and the rows with « Maître » or « à » kept in the key; no saved row is rewritten (AC3).
- **(b) One name saved both ways: decided (Lionel, 2026-10-21, Qb "A: Usual way's one (Rec.)").** The question as asked, word for word (the full text is in the story, Dev Notes "Decisions Recorded"): "10.20 Qb: one person saved twice, once each way. Which pseudonym do later documents use?" The options: "A: Usual way's one (Rec.)" — "Documents written the usual way, nearly all of them, keep exactly the pseudonym they get today. Only documents storing the accent apart change (« Anya Branco » for both)." / "B: The oldest one" — "The person keeps the first pseudonym they got. When the other way came first, usual documents change pseudonym (today's « Nassim Fonseca » would become « Noé Fonseca »)." So when a name and type are saved both ways, later documents use the pseudonym of the row saved the usual way; both rows stay saved, and an erasure by name removes both, each with its erasure entry (AC3).
- **(c) The detector and marks with no composed letter.** After AC1 the detector reads composed text, so a document storing its accents apart is detected exactly as the same document written the usual way, for every accented letter that has a composed form, which covers French: the « Mme Zo » cut goes away, and a name the usual form misses stays missed (on the PO's texts, 11 to 17 of 19 places found; one text loses two places found today). A mark with no composed letter (« x » + U+0301) would still stop the detector's patterns (`resources/detection_patterns.yaml:15`). Changing them is a detection change, not part of this story (G4); if the SM measures such a case, it is recorded as a follow-up for Lionel.

*Copied word for word from the epic at `106366e` (Open Point (a) after Lionel's P0-1). (a) and (b) are measured below and asked as Qa and Qb; (c) has its measurement in AC precisions, AC4, and stays a follow-up.*

### Open Points (a) and (b), measured, then decided

Decided by Lionel on 2026-10-21 (Decisions Recorded): **option 1** for (a) and **rule A** for (b). The measurements he decided on follow, unchanged; option 2 and rule B are not built.

SM probes `rgpd-analysis/r1020/sm/probes/p_cost.py`, `probes_pt/test_ab.py`, `probes_pt/test_misc.py`, `probes_pt/test_v220_make.py` (Reproduction Evidence); invented names; the real CLI with detection replaced.

**How a v2.2.0 database ends up with both (the PO's path 7), measured on v2.2.0** (`test_v220_make.py`, the v2.2.0 tree the PO archived, `r1019/po/nfd/v220`): `process` on « Le contrat avec Zórbalia Quentrel a signé. » composed, then decomposed: two rows, ('Zórbalia Quentrel', composed) « Anya Branco » and ('Zórbalia Quentrel', decomposed) « Lise Branco » (the last name « Branco » is shared because the last name « Quentrel » is the same in both forms, so the library reuses it; the first name differs). The reverse order gives ('Zórbalia Quentrel', decomposed) « Noé Fonseca », then ('Zórbalia Quentrel', composed) « Nassim Fonseca ». A decomposed « Mélora Quentrel » alone gives the row « lora Quentrel » after a lone U+0301, pseudonym « Eytan Roger », and the written « … avec MeEytan Roger a signé. ». The four databases are kept as `rgpd-analysis/r1020/sm/dbs/v220_{both,both_rev,nfd_only,mangled}.db` (passphrase `zorbalia_pass_A_123!`), converted to 1.1.0 on their first open by `43041e9` (10.7).

**Option 1, compare only (no stored data changes) — the design the prototype measures:**
- The repository reaches every form of a name by itself; no caller and no method signature changes. `find_by_full_name_and_type`, `find_all_by_full_name` (and so `delete_entities_by_full_name`) and `find_by_component` look up, in one indexed query (`IN`), the composed name, then the keys a row of it may hold from an earlier version (`former_keys`: `mapping_key`'s steps on the decomposed text, for each type the lookup covers, and the name as given). Because AES-SIV is deterministic, each candidate is one encryption and one index probe; nothing is decrypted that is not returned.
- **Cost, v1.1** (`probes/p_cost2.py` on the prototype v1.1, `logs/cost2_proto.txt`): the same databases as `p_cost.py`; 500 lookups of composed names drawn from the database; today's lookup reproduced in the same process as the one-key query `43041e9` makes, interleaved with option 1's, each the minimum of 7 rounds; the scan the minimum of 3, the rewrite the minimum of 3 on copies; three runs, their median shown:

  | Rows (decomposed / both ways) | Today, 500 lookups | Option 1, 500 lookups | Option 1 by scan, one scan | Option 2, rewrite once |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1,009 (81 / 9) | 0.111 s, 457 found | 0.162 s, 500 found | 0.038 s, misses the rows saved wrongly | 0.069 s; 81 rewritten, 9 left |
  | 10,087 (822 / 87) | 0.149 s, 471 found | 0.209 s, 500 found | 0.601 s, misses the rows saved wrongly | 0.425 s; 822 rewritten, 87 left |

  **The numbers Qa was asked with** (the PO's choice of the quieter run): v1.1's first separate-process run (`logs/cost_*_v3.txt`): today 0.110 s and 0.115 s, option 1 (with the « Maître » and « à » forms) 0.150 s and 0.153 s, one scan 0.034 s and 0.398 s, the rewrite 0.07 s and 0.28 s on `base` (0.06 s and 0.29 s on the prototype). The table above is a second measurement, on a busier machine, side by side in one process.

  Option 1 adds about 0.1 ms per lookup (0.090 to 0.152 ms over the three runs at 10,087 rows), whatever the database's size: it encrypts about six candidate keys instead of one since v1.1 (the « Maître » and « à » forms), and still makes one indexed query. A document makes about three lookups per name (the preview, the known-name split, the resolution), so a document of 50 names waits about 0.015 to 0.02 s more (an estimate from the measured cost). The machine was busier during the v1.1 runs than during v1.0's: v1.0's runs (option 1 without the « Maître » and « à » forms, separate processes, an idle machine; `logs/cost_*_v2.txt`) gave 0.108 s and 0.113 s today, 0.126 s and 0.144 s for option 1, 0.041 s and 0.397 s per scan, 0.05 s and 0.29 s for the rewrite; the separate-process runs of v1.1 (`logs/cost_*_v3.txt` to `_v7.txt`) vary by up to half under that load, which is why v1.1 measures option 1 and today side by side in one process. A scan once per lookup would cost the scan time for each lookup, so a scan belongs only once per document, cached; option 1's indexed query needs no cache.
- **What it reaches** (`test_ab.py`): a row saved decomposed (the composed document reuses « Emilien Roussel », no second row; `delete-mapping` with the composed name erases it, its entry written; `list-entities --search` lists it); a row that lost letters (the composed « Mélora Quentrel » reuses « Eytan Roger »; `delete-mapping "Mélora Quentrel"` erases « lora Quentrel » after a lone U+0301). A scan comparing composed forms reaches the first and not the second; the indexed query reaches both. Neither reaches a name saved in a form that mixes both ways inside the name (a document whose one name holds one accent each way), which no probe has met.
- **The rows saved with « Maître » or « à » in their key (the PO's M1), v1.1** (the PO's probe `r1020/po/probe/test_po_1020.py`, copied unchanged, its `test_new_reach` on the PO's eight databases `r1020/po/dbs/{v220,base}_{melora,maitre,maitre_plain,jerome}.db`, written by v2.2.0 and by `43041e9` from decomposed documents; `logs/po_m1_proto_new_reach.txt`): the old rows are « lora Quentrel » after a lone U+0301, « Maître Éloria Quentrel » and « Maître Vorlan Mestrix » with a decomposed « Maître » (and a decomposed « Éloria »), and « Je » + U+0301 + « ro » + U+0302 + « Quentrel ». On the prototype v1.0 the four « Maître » rows were missed (the PO's `logs/new_proto.txt`: « Le contrat avec Maître Autumn Xavier a signé. » beside the saved « Romy Xavier », a second row; `delete-mapping "Vorlan Mestrix"` exit 1, the row left). On v1.1, for all eight: a composed document reuses the old pseudonym (« Le contrat avec Maître Romy Xavier a signé. », « … Maître Lucas David … », « … Maître Grace Ventura … », « … Maître Mehdi Dumont … », « Léa Simões », « Lou Roque », « Charlie Weber », « Amaury Resende »), and `delete-mapping` with the name, with and without « Maître », erases the row (exit 0, nothing left): 16 of 16. T27 pins three such rows (« Maître Vorlan Mestrix », « Maître Éloria Quentrel », and the place « à Zórbaville »), T26 the fresh « Mélora ».
- **Collisions, v1.1:** the PO's check (`test_former_keys_collisions`, unchanged) on the prototype v1.1: over the app's 143,283 names (each of its 451 first names with each of its 316 last names, and each alone), 466,927 former keys (37,195 before the « Maître » forms), none equal to another name's key or decomposed key, and none shared by two names (`logs/po_m1_proto_collisions.txt`). The SM's same check for places (`probes/p_loc_collisions.py`): the app's 220 places, 1,143 former keys with the « à » forms, none (`logs/loc_collisions_proto.txt`).

**Option 2, rewrite once — designed and measured on a prototype function (not wired into `open_database`):**
- **When:** on the first open by 2.3.0, after the passphrase check (it needs the key to decrypt), after 10.7's conversion and 10.12's cleaning, in the same way: one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction on its own connection, all or nothing, a mark that it is done (a metadata row or a `PRAGMA user_version` value, as 10.12 marks its cleaning), re-checked inside the transaction for several processes.
- **How:** decrypt every row; for each whose full, first or last name is not composed, re-encrypt them composed and update the row; ids, types, pseudonyms and timestamps unchanged.
- **What the log records:** as 10.7 and 10.12 do, a log line with counts only (`names_composed`, rows rewritten, rows left, duration); no audit entry, never a name.
- **When both forms already exist (b):** the composed row and the decomposed row of one name and type cannot both be rewritten (the unique key on (text, type), 10.7); rewriting would mean deleting one of them, an erasure nobody asked for (the documents written with the dropped pseudonym could no longer be linked back). The prototype leaves such rows as they are: 9 of 1,009 and 87 of 10,087 in the measured databases.
- **What it cannot do:** repair a row that lost letters (its composed form is still « lora Quentrel » after a lone U+0301). So after option 2 the app still needs option 1's reach for the rows left and the rows that lost letters, and for the erasure of both forms (AC3). Cost: 0.069 s for 1,009 rows and 0.425 s for 10,087, once (v1.1's runs; 0.05 s and 0.29 s on v1.0's idle machine).

**Open Point (b), which pseudonym — measured on the prototype v1.1** (`test_ab.py`, with a measurement-only switch for the second rule, removed in v1.2; Lionel chose (A)):

| Database (v2.2.0) | Today (`43041e9`), composed document / decomposed document | (A) the composed row first | (B) the oldest first |
|---|---|---|---|
| `v220_both` (composed saved first) | « Anya Branco » / « Lise Branco » | « Anya Branco » / « Anya Branco » | « Anya Branco » / « Anya Branco » |
| `v220_both_rev` (decomposed saved first) | « Nassim Fonseca » / « Noé Fonseca » | « Nassim Fonseca » / « Nassim Fonseca » | « Noé Fonseca » / « Noé Fonseca » |

So (A) keeps what composed documents get today in every case; (B) changes it when the decomposed row is older. Either way: no second row, both rows kept; `delete-mapping "Zórbalia Quentrel"` lists both before the confirmation and erases both, 2 ERASURE entries (today: one, the composed row only, and « The pseudonym 'Anya Branco' is now permanently anonymous. » while « Lise Branco » stays); `delete-mapping --id` of one reports « 1 other row with the same name remains (type: PERSON). »; `import-mappings` from a database holding the decomposed row into one holding the composed row skips it as a duplicate (today: imported, a second row), and into an empty one saves it composed.

### Ordering and Rebase

The epic's rule, word for word from its Execution Sequence at `8bd21c0`:

**Stories 10.12 to 10.18, and 10.20,** are release blockers outside the chain: none changes detection code, so none has an accuracy baseline (10.20 makes one accuracy run as a check, its AC7). Each is its own PR, never shared with another story, and all eight land before the v2.3.0 release. Stories that change the same file are sequenced, and the later one is rebased on the earlier; they are never merged together. Most shared files are different functions, with two exceptions: 10.18 needs 10.15's code (its [A], the cells it gives the manual flow, and its F1 fix) and both change `_run_validation`; and 10.13, 10.15, 10.16 and 10.18 all edit the same `run_validation_workflow(…)` call (`core/document_processor.py:363-372`, its keyword arguments at `:368-370`), so every keyword argument is kept at each rebase. 10.20 goes after 10.16 and before 10.17 and 10.19; its shared files are in its Ordering. The shared files:
- 10.13, 10.15 and 10.18: `validation/workflow.py` and `validation/ui.py` (10.13 and 10.15 also `validation/models.py`); 10.18 reuses [A] as 10.15 leaves it.
- 10.13, 10.16 and 10.17 (and 10.14 if it changes it): `gui/models/validation_state.py`.
- 10.13 and 10.14: `gui/screens/validation.py` (`_on_finalize`'s batch branch) and the `gui/i18n/` files. The `.qm` files are binary: they are regenerated after a rebase, never merged.
- 10.15 and 10.18: `_run_validation` in `core/document_processor.py` (the same function). 10.16 shares the file and the `run_validation_workflow(…)` call's keyword arguments (`:368-370`), not the skip; its own points, as its SM measured them, are `:172-178`, `:368-370`, `:392-414`, `:416-482`, `:635-652` and `:737-739`. It does not change `:801` (Codex #5's save, in the backlog). 10.15 and 10.16 may also both change `validation/workflow.py`.
- Every story edits `CHANGELOG.md`; 10.12 shares no other file.

Suggested order: 10.12 at any time; 10.13, then 10.14, then 10.16, then 10.17 on the desktop side; 10.15 after 10.13, then 10.18 on the CLI side (every question is answered). 10.16 has no question and can go before 10.14 if needed.

**For 10.20 (SM):**
- **10.16 merges first, and 10.20 rebases on it** (the epic's Ordering). 10.16 is ready to merge (PR #109, head `4f6387e`); the dev starts after its merge (Task 0.2). 10.16's round 2 composes the texts its checks cut (`document_words`, the pseudonym's runs; its Fix Design, round 2). 10.20 keeps that code and, by AC4, changes `_WORD` as 10.16 leaves it. **Rebase rules:** in `core/custom_pseudonym.py`, keep 10.16's composed reading and 10.20's `_WORD`; in `core/document_processor.py` (10.16's custom-pseudonym points; 10.20's `_sanitize_error_message` only), keep both; in `gui/models/validation_state.py` and `gui/screens/validation.py`, 10.20 changes only the modify dialog's text (`:714`) and nothing of 10.16; `validation/workflow.py` (10.16 near [C]; 10.20 `_locate_modified_span` and one import), keep both. Any other conflict is a stop-and-report.
- **10.17 and 10.19 rebase on 10.20** (both approved, not merged). Each adds a decomposed-accent guard case to its own tests (a decomposed document and a composed typed name: every place reached, never an "Added" while a place stays in clear; path 9). 10.19's forms rule (its `is_form`, `form_places`) builds on 10.20's boundaries (`validation/models.py`, `not_after_word()` and `not_before_word()`): its `form_places` takes 10.20's mark class in its lookarounds, and its "accents set aside" comparison (Lionel's 10.19 Q3) reads composed text. 10.19's `delete-mapping` by name across capitals builds on 10.20's repository (every form of the name); its approved screen line replaces Qc's line.
- **10.18 has merged** (`43041e9`): its question flow types through `get_text_input`, so a name typed there is composed too; no 10.18 test types a decomposed name.
- **The rule:** never merged together. Whichever merges second rebases on `main`, re-runs its tests-first proof on the rebased tests-first commit, gets CI green and (10.20) a new accuracy run on the rebased head, the read-only Codex review on the rebased head, and QA re-checks the rebased diff before the merge command. `CHANGELOG.md`: keep every story's entry, in merge order.

### Gates G1–G7 (verbatim from the epic)

[Source: prd/epic-10-v2.3-detection-precision-quick-wins.md#Gates]

The following block is normative and is referenced by every story's acceptance criteria.

- **G1 — Metric evidence.** Every reported metric comes from a CI accuracy run on the story branch: `gh workflow run accuracy.yaml --ref <branch> -f reason="<story id>"`. Cite the run ID. Paste the `[Overall]`, `[PERSON]`, `[LOCATION]`, `[ORG]` lines from the `accuracy-results` artifact (`accuracy-output.txt`). Numbers typed by the dev agent without a run ID do not count.
- **G2 — QA verifies independently.** The QA agent downloads the cited run's artifact itself (`gh run download <id> -n accuracy-results`) and checks the numbers match the story's claims. QA never accepts the dev's text as evidence.
- **G3 — Recall guard.** Overall recall and each per-type recall must not drop versus the previous merged baseline. Any drop: stop and ask Lionel.
- **G4 — Attribution.** Changes to annotations/scorer and changes to detection code are never in the same PR. Benchmark-driven deltas and detector-driven deltas are reported separately.
- **G5 — Held-out check.** Once the held-out set exists (story 10.1), every detector story also reports held-out P/R/F1. A gain on the main corpus with a loss on held-out is a red flag for overfitting: report it.
- **G6 — Normal quality.** black, ruff, mypy clean; unit tests for every new rule; full CI green (one re-run allowed for the known macOS flake).
- **G7 — Docs.** If a story changes the headline numbers, it updates README/README.fr, `docs/faq*.md`, `docs/index*.md`, `docs/tutorial*.md`, the QA report and CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]`, all from the G1 run.

#### Epic 10 G7 interpretation (Lionel, 2026-10-02)

Decided by Lionel, 2026-10-02. This note interprets the gates for Epic 10; the gate text above is unchanged.

- **G7 — per story vs. close-out.** The QA report (`docs/qa/ner-accuracy-report.md`) records the before/after at every story (10.1 marked "benchmark repair, same detector"), and each story adds its CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` entry. README/README.fr, `docs/faq*.md`, `docs/index*.md` and `docs/tutorial*.md` change once, at Epic 10 close-out, from the final G1 run — not per story.
- **G3 under a ground-truth change (10.1).** G3 does not block 10.1. 10.1's close-out run becomes the new baseline. Every per-type recall drop between the old and new ground truth is listed with its cause (e.g. "+N ORG annotations added") in the story and the PR. G3 applies strictly from 10.2 onwards.

**How they apply to 10.20 (AC11):**
- **G1:** AC7, one regular accuracy run on the branch at the final code (Task 6.2): the 16 lines identical to `38212303870`, cited by ID, pasted in the PR. The PR states that the detector's code and patterns do not change.
- **G3 and G5:** read from the AC7 run's 16 lines (main corpus and held-out). Identical lines: no recall change, no held-out change. Any recall drop: stop and ask Lionel.
- **G2:** QA downloads the AC7 artifact itself, re-runs the AC6 tests and the PO's probes, checks out the tests-first commit, reads the diff (Task 7).
- **G4:** never in the same PR as a detector change, or as another Epic 10 story (Task 6.1).
- **G6:** in full; reruns for infrastructure failures only.
- **G7:** no headline number changes; the CHANGELOG entry only.
- **No skip marker anywhere** (Task 6.5).

### Product Constraints (non-negotiable, verbatim from the epic)

[Source: prd/epic-10-v2.3-detection-precision-quick-wins.md#Product Constraints]

These apply to every story in this epic. A story that cannot meet its goal inside them stops and goes back to Lionel; it does not relax them.

1. **Local NLP only.** No LLM, no generative model, no API call, no cloud service, no telemetry. Every change is a deterministic rule, lexicon or pattern that runs offline.
2. **No encoder NER models.** Introducing a transformer/encoder NER model (e.g. CamemBERT-NER) is a Lionel decision that belongs to Epic 9. It must not be introduced in this epic, including as an optional dependency or experiment committed to the repo.
3. **Mandatory human validation stays.** No auto-accept mode, no `--no-validate`, no confidence-based skipping. Precision gains reduce the reviewer's workload; they do not replace the reviewer.
4. **No user data** for training, tuning or evaluation. All corpus and held-out documents are synthetic.
5. **Recall over precision.** Missing a real person is worse than showing an extra candidate. Any drop in overall recall or in any per-type recall needs Lionel's sign-off (gate G3).
6. **No public accuracy claim without CI evidence.** Every number that reaches README, FAQ, docs, CHANGELOG or the QA report comes from a cited CI accuracy run (gate G1).

For 10.20: constraints 1, 2 and 4 hold (a deterministic composed form from Python's own Unicode database, no model, no data). Constraint 3: the review is unchanged; a name written with a separate accent now reaches it like any other. Constraint 5: the detector receives composed text; on the PO's ten sentences that finds more than today, and AC7's run must show the scores unchanged (the corpus is read by the accuracy suite's own loader). Constraint 6: the CHANGELOG entry carries no number.

### Cross-Story Rules (verbatim from the epic)

[Source: prd/epic-10-v2.3-detection-precision-quick-wins.md#Cross-Story Rules]

These notes sit outside the normative Gates block above and do not change its text. They bind every detector story from 10.3a on: 10.3a, 10.3b, 10.3c and 10.4 (10.3c added, orchestrator 2026-10-07; rule text unchanged).

- **Coverage level 2b (Lionel, 2026-10-06):** No rule may remove the last covering span of any type from an annotation of any type without Lionel's sign-off.

For 10.20: the rule binds detector stories; 10.20 changes no detection code (G4; Task 6.1), and AC7's run is its check.

### Isolation Rules

These apply to the dev agent (the BMAD dev agent, Claude), the orchestrator, the QA agent and the PO.

The blocks below are copied word for word from the 10.11 story at `8bd21c0` (Dev Notes "Isolation Rules"), which copies them from the 10.10, 10.8, 10.6 and 10.4 stories. The task and AC references inside are those stories'; "How they bind 10.20" maps them to this story.

**Standing rules, word for word from the 10.6 story** (Dev Notes "Isolation Rules", "Standing rules (word for word, as given for every story from 10.6 on …)", at `main` `4f3cf30`):

- **Held-out isolation:** no agent opens, lists, reads or greps anything under `tests/test_corpus/held_out/`. Held-out results are used only as aggregate lines in a CI `accuracy-results` artifact. Any `tests/unit/held_out_leakage_allowlist.json` change needs Lionel's recorded approval.
- **Final-exam isolation, from the start of 10.6 authoring:** no agent opens, lists or greps the final-exam material (isolated workspace or `tests/test_corpus/final_exam/`). Orchestrating agents handle only paths, hashes and counts. Final-exam numbers are never used for a decision or for tuning.
- **Held-out-blind strings:** any real string in a new test or resource must occur in the main-corpus text or be Lionel-approved. Use invented names: Zorbalia, Quentrix, Zorbaville, Quentrel ("Quentin" is real).
- **CI must be all green to merge, and a red check is fixed inside the current PR.** Reruns are for infrastructure failures only. Absolute wall-clock test asserts are banned.
- **Recall over precision, and the coverage invariant:** not applicable to PR A, but restated so later stories keep them (see "Product Constraints and Cross-Story Rules" below).

- The leakage guard `tests/unit/test_held_out_leakage.py` (non-slow, runs in normal CI) fails if a held-out-only entity string appears in `gdpr_pseudonymizer/resources/**` or `tests/unit/**` (`SEARCH_TREES`, `:34`). This story adds files to both trees (new resources, new tests, edited `detection_patterns.yaml`). The guard is the only check that your strings and list entries are not held-out strings.
- **Allowlist:** `tests/unit/held_out_leakage_allowlist.json` is keyed by (path, sha256). Any change to it needs **Lionel's recorded approval in this story** (date + what). QA flags any PR diff on it.
- **If the leakage guard fires:** the message gives a path and a hash only. Do not try to find out which string it is.
  - **Unit test file with invented strings only:** replace your synthetic string with another clearly invented one.
  - **Unit test file that contains AC-named or other real strings:** treat it like a resource. Do **not** bisect, edit or remove strings to find the hit. HALT and send the whole file (path, hash, list of its real strings) to Lionel.
  - **A resource file** (stoplist, role words, particles, AC3 lexicon, `detection_patterns.yaml`): do **not** remove, edit or bisect entries, because each removal would reveal which entry is a held-out string. HALT and send the **whole frozen list** (or the lexicon reference) to Lionel with the path and hash. Lionel either allowlists it (path + hash + date recorded here and in the allowlist) or drops the category concerned. Record his decision in Decisions Recorded.
  - Either way, note it in Completion Notes by hash only.

- **Calibration (Lionel, 2026-10-09):** the guards target real channels: text actually in a prompt or a file, public CI logs, and the held-out and final-exam access rules. No guard assumes that a model session remembers anything across calls; each GPT or Gemini call starts from fresh context.

**How they bind 10.20:**
- The search scope is Task 0.4, which also keeps `tests/accuracy/` closed (the brief's rule for this story); the string check is Task 1.5; any allowlist change needs Lionel's recorded approval in this story.
- 10.20 adds two test files to `tests/unit/` and no resource; both leakage guards scan them on every CI run.
- The final exam stays closed; the dev agent's brief names no held-out or final-exam path. AC7's run reads the corpus on CI only; nobody opens it.
- The new files hold invented names only (AC15), main-corpus words (pre-checked, Test Plan), the command's own confirmation word « yes » (a code identifier of `delete_mapping.py:150`, already in `tests/unit/test_config_manager.py` at `43041e9`; the blind check lists it as needing approval; **approved by the PO in the review of v1.0, as the command's own confirmation word**), an invented passphrase, an invented sheet name, file names and code identifiers. No combining mark is written in a test file.
- **The user's settings file is never touched:** every test points `HOME`, `USERPROFILE` and the current folder at its `tmp_path` and `gui.config._config_path` into it; every `.bat` runner sets `USERPROFILE` and `HOME` to a scratch folder. Never touch `C:\Users\devea\.gdpr-pseudo.yaml`.
- **No absolute wall-clock assert**, no sleep; the desktop tests wait on a condition with a generous timeout that only stops a hang. The cost numbers of Open Point (a) are measurements for Lionel's question, never test assertions.

### Fix Design (pre-decided where Lionel decided; Qa, Qb and Qc open)

The SM prototype implements it (`proto_1020.diff`; Reproduction Evidence); it is a measurement file. The dev agent writes the real code from this text.

**One module (`gdpr_pseudonymizer/utils/text_form.py`, new; no import of the package).**
1. `composed(text: str) -> str`: `unicodedata.normalize("NFC", text)`.
2. `is_mark(char: str) -> bool`: `unicodedata.combining(char) != 0` (AC4's rule).
3. `mark_class() -> str`: the body of a regex character class holding every combining mark of the running Python's Unicode database, as escaped ranges, built once on first use (`functools.lru_cache`; 0.04 s to scan every code point, measured), so it matches the `\w` of the same Python.
4. `word_pattern()` (cached): `[\w<marks>]+`; `not_after_word()`: `(?<![\w<marks>\-‐‑])`; `not_before_word()`: `(?![\w<marks>\-‐‑])` (10.15's hyphens kept).

**AC1, the readers (`utils/file_handler.py`, `utils/tabular_reader.py`).**
1. `read_file`'s plain-text branch returns `composed(f.read())`; `read_pdf` returns `composed(extracted)` (the scanned-PDF warning still counts the extracted characters); `read_docx`, `read_excel`, `read_csv` return their joined text composed.
2. `read_excel_structured` and `read_csv_structured`: each `CellData.value` is `composed(...)`; sheet names, rows, columns and cell references unchanged.
3. No other change: the encodings, the CSV sniffing, the docx and xlsx traversal, the errors.

**AC2, the typed texts.**
1. `validation/ui.py` `get_text_input`: `return composed(Prompt.ask(prompt_text))`.
2. `cli/commands/delete_mapping.py`: the name, when given, composed before it is used (`delete_mapping_command`'s start).
3. `list-entities --search`: composed where it is compared (`search_entities`, AC3); `list-mappings --search`: `composed(search)` compared with `composed(e.full_name)` and `composed(e.pseudonym_full)` (`:134-140`).
4. Desktop: `_show_modify_text_dialog` composes the dialog's text before comparing it with the current text and locating it; `_show_change_pseudonym_dialog` needs nothing (`normalize_custom_pseudonym` → `mapping_key`, AC3); `gui/screens/database.py` composes the search field's text (`:555`) and compares it with the composed name and pseudonym (`:574-580`); `gui/workers/database_worker.py` the same (`:230-236`); `gui/widgets/entity_panel.py` keeps the filter composed (`:441-443`), compared with the composed entity text.
5. Not composed: passphrases, paths, `--reason`, the confirmation word (AC13).

**AC3, the comparisons with saved names.**
1. `core/mapping_key.py`: `mapping_key` composes its text, then applies today's steps (titles, LOCATION prepositions, spaces). A new function `former_keys(entity_text, entity_type) -> list[str]`: the keys an earlier version could have saved for this text read decomposed: today's steps on `NFD(entity_text)`; `NFD(mapping_key(...))`; and (the PO's M1, v1.1) today's steps on `NFD(f"{word} {key}")` for each `word` of `("Maître", "maître", "MAÎTRE")`, for every type, and of `("à", "À")` for a LOCATION, where `key` is `mapping_key(...)`; without duplicates and without the composed key. Two module constants hold those words, the only accented words of `FRENCH_TITLE_PATTERN` and `FRENCH_PREPOSITION_PATTERN`, with a comment saying so (a new accented title or preposition there needs its word here). It is what reaches the rows saved wrongly: « lora Quentrel » after a lone U+0301 for « Mélora Quentrel », « Maître Vorlan Mestrix » (decomposed « Maître ») for « Vorlan Mestrix », « à Zórbaville » (decomposed) for the place « Zórbaville ». The repository applies it to the key it is handed (item 2); the callers do not change (AC precisions, AC3, "The SM's choice, and why").
2. **Option 1 (Lionel's Qa, "A: Find as they are (Rec.)"):** `data/repositories/mapping_repository.py` imports `former_keys` (`core/mapping_key.py` imports nothing of `data/`: no cycle) and reaches every form in one query, signatures unchanged: `find_by_full_name_and_type` (`IN` over the composed name, its `former_keys` for that type and the name as given, with the type), `_query_by_full_name` and so `find_all_by_full_name` and `delete_entities_by_full_name` (the same for every type), `find_by_component` (the component's forms as a PERSON). `search_entities` compares composed forms. `_save_batch_with_retry`'s lookup follows (`:398`).
3. **No saved row is rewritten** (Lionel's Qa): no conversion on open, no change to `data/database.py`; the rows saved decomposed or saved wrongly keep their stored names (G6).
4. **Which row of a name saved both ways (Lionel's Qb, "A: Usual way's one (Rec.)"):** the rows found are ordered with the composed name first, then the former keys in their order; the first is used (T29). `find_all_by_full_name` keeps its order by type, each type's rows in that order.
5. `cli/commands/import_mappings.py`: the new row's `full_name`, `first_name` and `last_name` composed (the duplicate check is option 1's lookup). (Superseded in v1.14: copied as the source saved them; Fix Design, "QA's round 4".)
6. Nothing else of the data layer changes: the schema, the unique key, `find_all`, `delete_entity_by_id`, `save`, `save_batch`.

**AC3, the searches (v1.5; QA's SEARCH-001, the orchestrator's ruling (a); lines at `87cd011`).** The dev agent writes it from this text; the SM's prototype (`proto_1020_v14.diff`, on `87cd011`) is a measurement file.
1. `core/mapping_key.py`, after `former_keys` (`:63-97`): a new function `search_forms(search_text: str, entity_type: str) -> list[str]`. It returns the composed search (`composed(search_text)`), then each of `former_keys(search_text, entity_type)` composed, without one equal to `mapping_key(search_text, entity_type)` unless it is the composed search itself, each lowercased, without duplicates. The rule on the key keeps a search's titles and prepositions as today (AC13): for « Mme Éloria » (PERSON) it gives « mme éloria » and « maître éloria », never « éloria ». For « Mélora Quentrel » it gives « mélora quentrel », a lone U+0301 + « lora quentrel », and « maître » + a space + a lone U+0301 + « lora quentrel ».
2. The four searches each build the forms once per search, for the two kinds of type (`search_forms(search, "PERSON")` and `search_forms(search, "LOCATION")`), and keep a row when one of its type's forms (LOCATION's for a LOCATION row, PERSON's for any other, as `_query_by_full_name`, `mapping_repository.py:257-281`) is a substring of the row's `composed(full_name).lower()`. The comparison with the pseudonym stays as it is (the composed, lowercased search in the composed, lowercased pseudonym):
   - `data/repositories/mapping_repository.py:582-589`, in `search_entities` (`:560`): `list-entities --search` (`cli/commands/list_entities.py:111`), with or without `--type`;
   - `cli/commands/list_mappings.py:135-142`: `list-mappings --search`;
   - `gui/screens/database.py:575-584`, in `_apply_filters_inline` (`:570`): up to 200 saved names (`_SEARCH_BACKGROUND_THRESHOLD`, `:55`);
   - `gui/workers/database_worker.py:230-238`, in `_search_entities` (`:212`): over 200; the screen hands it the search already composed (`database.py:557`).
3. Nothing else changes: `former_keys`, the lookups, `delete-mapping`, the type filters, the order of the rows, the messages. No saved row is rewritten.
4. Measured on the prototype (Reproduction Evidence, item 15): T31, T32 and G8 to G13 pass; the desktop worker's search over 10,001 saved names takes about a hundredth of a second more (0.015 to 0.019 s against 0.006 s).

**The Codex review of `186972b` (v1.7; the orchestrator's ruling `rgpd-analysis/r1020/codex/ruling.md`; lines at `186972b`).** The dev agent writes it from this text; the SM's prototype (`proto_1020_v16.diff`, option A, Lionel's answer: 5 files, +129 −51, ruff, black and mypy clean) is a measurement file; `pB/` (option B) is history.
1. **`core/mapping_key.py:63-97`, `former_keys`, becomes a replay of what an earlier version saved.** A constant `MAX_MIXED_WORDS = 4`. `_key_texts(key, entity_type)`: the words of today's key; every per-word mix of their composed and decomposed forms (`itertools.product`, every word decomposed first) when at most `MAX_MIXED_WORDS` words carry an accent, else the two whole forms; then each of the two whole forms with `NFD(word)` inserted at each of the `len(words) + 1` positions for each word of `FORMER_TITLE_WORDS`, and for a LOCATION `NFD("à")` and `NFD("À")` in front. `_former(entity_text, entity_type)`: for each text, `_key_steps(" ".join(words), entity_type)`; skipped when empty, equal to today's key or already found; equal to the joined text: "whole", or "titled" when a word was inserted; else "sign" when one of its words starts with a combining mark (`unicodedata.combining`) or it has fewer words than the text; else "cut". **P0-1 (Lionel, 2026-10-22, "A: Only with a sign (Rec.)"):** the "cut" keys are dropped here. `former_keys` returns the keys in that order, today's key left out.
2. **`saved_name_forms(entity_text, entity_type)`**, new, beside it: the composed text; the old keys; `composed(...)` of each "titled" and "sign" key (Codex P1-3); the text as given; without duplicates, in that order (rule A: the composed text first). v1.18: without the `composed(...)` forms (the third Codex review's P0-b).
3. **`core/mapping_key.py:100-125`, `search_forms`:** the composed forms of the old keys other than "cut" (T41); the rest as in v1.5.
4. **`data/repositories/mapping_repository.py:23-32`, `_name_forms`:** returns `saved_name_forms(full_name, entity_type)`; the queries, the ranks and rule A unchanged.
5. **`core/custom_pseudonym.py:557-561` (Q1, Codex P1-2):** the rows of `entity_type` whose `full_name` is one of `saved_name_forms(name, entity_type)`; if any, the refusal names the pseudonym of the one whose form ranks first.
6. **`core/custom_pseudonym.py:601-604` (Q-C2, Codex P0-2):** `stored_names` also holds `fold(mapping_key(row.full_name, row.entity_type))` for every row; a second set holds each row's `full_name` casefolded with its spaces collapsed, accent marks kept. A run is refused when `fold(run)` is in the first set, or when one of `saved_name_forms(run, "PERSON")` and `saved_name_forms(run, "LOCATION")`, casefolded with its spaces collapsed, is in the second. Set time and finalization call the same function.
7. **The writers (Codex P2; AC5):** `utils/file_handler.py:368` writes `composed(content)`; `utils/tabular_writer.py:65`, `:101` and `:150` write `composed(cell.value)`. The replacement (`core/document_processor.py:909`) keeps the stored pseudonym; the writer composes it.
8. **Not changed:** `cli/commands/import_mappings.py:194-201` (it keeps composing; P1-3's choice, AC precisions; superseded in v1.14, IMPORT-002); the schema; no stored row is rewritten; the readers; the detector; every text the user reads.

### Test Plan

Two new files written by the BMAD dev agent (Claude): `tests/unit/test_accents_one_form_cli.py` (T1-T10, T12-T18, T20-T23, T26-T30, G2-G7) drives the real readers, the real `gdpr-pseudo process`, `batch --workers 1`, `delete-mapping`, `list-entities` and `list-mappings` commands; `tests/unit/gui/test_accents_one_form_desktop.py` (T11, T19, T24, T25, G1) drives the real desktop single-document and batch paths, the dialogs, the database screen and the review's filter. Invented names only; a database per test (`init_database`, passphrase `zorbalia_pass_A_123!` from `GDPR_PSEUDO_PASSPHRASE`). **Preconditions are `pytest.fail("PRE: …")` calls** (also in the helpers), never `assert`; the first `assert` after them is the expected failure on the unfixed code.

**Harness (CLI)**, as 10.15's and 10.19's: `typer.testing.CliRunner().invoke(app, …, env={"GDPR_PSEUDO_PASSPHRASE": …}, catch_exceptions=False)`; `readchar.readkey` from a list (Enter passes the summary screen; Space after the list ends); `rich.prompt.Prompt.ask` from a list (typed texts; « yes » for `delete-mapping`'s confirmation); `rich.prompt.Confirm.ask` answers n to 10.18's « Add a name the detector missed … » and y otherwise. **Detection replaced below the reader:** `DocumentProcessor._get_detector` and `_detect_and_filter_entities` by a stand-in that finds every occurrence of each (name, type) given **in either form** (its composed and its decomposed spelling), in the text it receives; so a path behaves the same on the unfixed and the fixed code up to the reader. T3 replaces detection by the real regex layer (`RegexMatcher()`, `load_patterns()`, `match_entities`), the layer that cuts « Mme Zo », with no spaCy model, so it runs on every leg; T4 uses the real detector (`spacy`). Rows: `open_database` + `SQLiteMappingRepository.find_all()`. ERASURE entries: the `operations` table read with `sqlite3`. A row "saved by an earlier version" is written with the repository's `save` and the exact name given (T14-T19): `save` writes the name as given, today and after the fix (Fix Design, AC3, item 6); new rows are composed upstream, by `mapping_key`. Settings: `HOME`, `USERPROFILE` and the current folder at `tmp_path`, `gui.config._config_path` into it.
**Harness (desktop)**, as 10.17's and the PO's: `DetectionWorker` run synchronously with `DocumentProcessor._detect_and_filter_entities` replaced (not `detect_entities`, so the document goes through `read_file`); `ValidationScreen.start_validation`; the batch through the real batch worker with `_detect_and_filter_entities` replaced; a correction through the screen's own `_show_modify_text_dialog(<row id>)` with `QInputDialog.getText` returning the typed text, and a custom pseudonym through `_show_change_pseudonym_dialog`; pending rows accepted; `_on_finalize`; the written text from the results screen's temporary output. Every `ConfirmDialog` answers yes.
**Files:** `.docx` with python-docx; `.xlsx` and `.csv` with openpyxl and `csv` (header « Nom », « Note », a cell « ok »); a PDF whose accents are drawn apart without a font file: the standard Helvetica font, WinAnsi encoding, and a `/ToUnicode` map from the bytes of the spacing accents (0xB4, 0x60, 0x88, 0xA8, 0xB8) to U+0301, U+0300, U+0302, U+0308, U+0327 (pdfplumber extracts « o » then U+0301; measured; the SM's `sm20_pdf.py` is one way to write it; the dev writes its own).

**Strings.** Invented: « Zórbalia », « Quentrel », « Zórbalia Quentrel », « Vorlan Mestrix », « Zorbal Quentrix », « Zox » + U+0301 + « rbalia » (`unicodedata.lookup`), « Xantor Vellion », « Xantor Véllion », « Xantor Quovix », « Xantor Zoxrbalia », « Zorba » + « ﬁ » + « x » (G4, written `\ufb01`), « Mélora Quentrel » (T26, T28), « Zorbôme Quentrel » (T28), « Éloria Quentrel », « Zórbaville », « Xantorville » (T27, T30), « quentrel quovan », « Zorbal Quentrix » (G7); v1.5: « Zórbalia Mestrix », « Quentrel Zórbalia », « Vorlan Zórbalia » (G12), the passphrase « Zórbalia_pass_A_123! » (G8); v1.12: « Zorbal-à-Quentrix », « Zorbal-Quentrix » (G16; its word « à » is a checked main-corpus word; the blind check gives `checked=5 not_in_main_corpus=4`: the two strings and their two invented words). Real words, all in the main corpus but the last (`scripts/accuracy_heldout_blind_check.py`, `checked=17 not_in_main_corpus=1`): Le, le, contrat, avec, a, été, signé, Signé, valide, Mme, Nom, Note, ok, Maître, à, et; « yes », the command's own confirmation word, approved by the PO (Isolation Rules). The v1.5 tests add no real word: theirs (Le, contrat, avec, a, été, signé, Mme, Maître) give `checked=8 not_in_main_corpus=0`. VM is « Vorlan Mestrix », ZO « Zórbalia », ZQ « Zórbalia Quentrel ». SENT = « Le contrat avec Zórbalia Quentrel a été signé. »; A_TEXT = « Vorlan Mestrix valide. Le contrat avec Zórbalia. Signé : Zórbalia. »; E_TEXT = « Zórbalia Quentrel a signé. Signé : Zórbalia Quentrel. »; R_TEXT = « Le contrat avec Zórbalia Quentrel a signé. »; MIXED = « Zórbalia Quentrel a signé. » composed, then « Signé : Zórbalia Quentrel. » decomposed. "nfd(x)" is `unicodedata.normalize("NFD", x)`. **v1.16, the words « Maitre » and « MAITRE » (T50, v1.14):** the title « Maître » without its circumflex, not in the main corpus; approved by Lionel on 2026-10-22, the question word for word from the decision log ("Story 10.20 test words « Maitre » / « MAITRE »"): "May 10.20's tests use the words « Maitre » and « MAITRE » (the title « Maître » without its circumflex)?" — **Lionel: "Approve (Rec.)"**. The blind check of T50's words: `checked=5 not_in_main_corpus=2`, the two approved.

### Existing Tests

The suites that exercise these paths and must stay green: `tests/unit/test_file_handler_formats.py`, `tests/integration/test_pdf_docx_processing.py`, the tabular readers' and writers' tests, `tests/unit/test_mapping_repository*.py`, `test_type_aware_mapping_repository.py`, `tests/unit/cli/commands/test_delete_mapping*.py`, `test_list_entities*.py`, `test_list_mappings*.py`, `test_import_mappings*.py`, `tests/unit/test_erasure_log_no_name.py`, `tests/integration/test_erasure_workflow.py`, 10.15's `test_cli_corrections_hand_added.py`, 10.18's `test_cli_no_review_add.py`, the custom-pseudonym files of 10.11 and 10.16, `tests/unit/gui/`.

- **At `43041e9`: no existing test changes** (Reproduction Evidence, item 4: the same 2534 passes on both trees). No existing test or doc holds `delete-mapping`'s old several-row line, so Lionel's Qc changes none.

Their refusals, messages, guards and outcomes stay as 10.16 measured them; round-1 files do not change unless a round-2 test's helper there needs one of these edits. The dev re-measures at Task 0.6 and 3.2; an edit for any other reason is a stop-and-report.

Measured on both prototypes: without these edits these four fail and every other existing test passes; with them the file passes, 11 of 11. AC5's reason, which AC6 allows in 10.16's round-2 files.

### CHANGELOG Entry (Task 5)

`### Fixed`, a new bullet (AC8; the PM may prefer other wording; the two sentences on saved names follow Lionel's Qa and Qb, and since v1.8 his P0-1: « still found … erased, except a name that lost its last letters », the epic's AC8 at `315eb24`; and since v1.11 his end-cut answer: the refusal sentence, the epic's AC8 at `9f0ccd5`):

- **Names whose accents are stored as a separate mark (Story 10.20).** Some PDFs and programs store an accented letter as the plain letter followed by a separate accent mark; it looks the same on screen, but the app treated such a name as a different name from the same name written the usual way. Such a name could be cut by the detector (« Mme Zórbalia » found as « Mme Zo », the end of the name left in the written file), keep the letters « Me » of a name such as « Mélora » in clear before its pseudonym, be reported « not found » with [A], be left partly in clear by a correction (in the command line and in both desktop reviews), be shown as two names in the review, get a second pseudonym in a later file, and not be found, or be only partly erased, by `delete-mapping`; the searches of `list-entities`, `list-mappings` and the desktop app did not find it. All of this was already so in v2.2.0, except the « Mme Zo » cut, which was never released. Every file read, every name you type and every comparison with your saved names now use the usual (composed) form of accented letters, so a document storing its accents apart is now detected exactly as the same document written the usual way. Written files therefore come out in that form: the same look, different bytes, also where no name was replaced. Names an earlier version saved with a separate mark, or saved wrongly because of it (« Maître » kept in front, letters lost), are still found, reused and erased, except a name that lost its last letters; nothing in your database is rewritten. A custom pseudonym equal to a name an earlier version saved wrongly, a name cut at its end included, is refused. When the same name was saved both ways, later files use the pseudonym saved the usual way; `delete-mapping` with the name lists and erases both, and its screen now reads « You are about to delete the following N saved pseudonyms (all of this name, whatever its type): ». Detection, the database schema and the accuracy figures do not change.

### CI Facts

- **`ci.yaml`:** Ubuntu with Python 3.11 (coverage) and 3.10, macOS with 3.12, Windows with 3.12; `pytest -m "not slow" -n auto --dist loadscope --timeout=300` on Linux and macOS, `-m "not spacy and not slow"` on Windows; `QT_QPA_PLATFORM: offscreen`; the `formats gui` extras (pdfplumber, python-docx, openpyxl, PySide6) and fpdf2 (dev). The new files are unmarked but T4 and need no font file and no spaCy model, so they run on every leg, under xdist (the SM ran its sketch under `-n 4`). Python 3.10 has Unicode 13.0 and 3.12 Unicode 15.0: `mark_class()` is built from the running Python, as `\w` is. Lint: `ruff check .`, `black --check .`, `mypy gdpr_pseudonymizer`.
- **Config:** `pytest.ini` (`--strict-markers`, `filterwarnings = error` except `UserWarning` and `DeprecationWarning`); the SM's sketch ran under the same filter (the batch screen's known disconnect warning filtered in the sketch, as the PO's probes do).
- **`accuracy.yaml`:** the AC7 run, dispatched by hand (`workflow_dispatch`, `-f reason="10.20"`); its `accuracy-results` artifact holds `accuracy-output.txt`.
- **After the merge:** the fix touches `core/`, so a performance run fires on `main` and must be green.

### Environment

- **The dev agent and the Codex review (AC10; Lionel, 2026-10-18).** The BMAD dev agent (Claude) writes the tests first (Task 1), then the fix (Task 2) and every later code fix. No other agent writes code on this branch, and no `codex` session that writes (`workspace-write`) runs on it. The orchestrator runs the tests, the lint, the commits, the pushes and the accuracy run, and reads every diff before using it. **The final review (Task 6.3)** is one read-only session run by the orchestrator on the PR's final head: `codex exec --sandbox read-only` over `git diff main...HEAD`, with a prompt that asks for correctness, names or parts of names left in clear when written with a separate accent, every reader and typed entry composed, the saved-name lookups by Lionel's Qa and Qb, the word cuts keeping marks, nothing changed in the detector, and conformance to this story's Fix Design and Qc's text, and ends with a VERDICT line; its session id, model, sandbox and VERDICT line go in the Debug Log. It runs again only if the code changes after it. If `codex` is missing, out of quota or fails, stop and tell Lionel.
- **The measurement-files rule.** `proto_1020.diff`, the trees `base/`, `proto/`, `t16/`, `p16/`, and every file of `sketch/`, `probes/`, `probes_pt/`, `tools/` and `dbs/` in `rgpd-analysis/r1020/sm/` are Claude-written measurement files, like the PO's probes (`r1019/po/nfd/`). They stay measurement only: never committed, never applied in the worktree. The dev agent writes its own code and tests from this story's Fix Design, Test Plan and AC precisions, plus the isolation rules, and from the repository. The PR body says "code and tests written by the BMAD dev agent (Claude); design and measurement sketches by the SM (Claude); final diff reviewed read-only by `codex exec`" (Task 6.5).
- **The worktree has no toolchain.** Use the main checkout's Poetry environment (`C:\Users\devea\AppData\Local\pypoetry\Cache\virtualenvs\gdpr-pseudonymizer-_kRLwMSQ-py3.11`) through `.bat` files run with `cmd.exe /c`: `cd /d` to a scratch folder (the lint runners `cd` to the worktree), `set PYTHONPATH=C:\Users\devea\Documents\GitHub\rgpd-wt-s1020`, `set QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen`, `set PYTHONUTF8=1`, `set PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, **`set USERPROFILE=C:\Users\devea\Documents\GitHub\rgpd-analysis\r1020\dev\home` and `set HOME=` the same folder** (every runner), then `<env>\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -c <worktree>\pytest.ini --rootdir <worktree> -p no:cacheprovider …`. The pattern is in `rgpd-analysis\r1020\sm\run_check.bat` and `run_suite.bat`; `r1020\sm\plugins\sm_isolation.py` points `gui.config._config_path` at `tmp_path` (`-p`). A `cmd.exe /c` string cannot carry `-m "not slow"` and mangles accented arguments: use `.bat` files and UTF-8 input files. Never `pip install` or `poetry install` from WSL. The full suite builds the docs into `site/` in the worktree (`tests/integration/test_documentation_build.py`, gitignored): remove it after the run.

### Previous Story Insights

- **10.16 (round 2, not merged):** its checks read their texts composed before cutting them into words, and record a limit, its follow-up (f): a mark with no composed letter is still cut out of its word. 10.20 closes (f) (AC4) and composes everything the checks receive. Its round-2 tests encode decomposed bytes and change here for that reason (Existing Tests).
- **10.15 (merged):** [A] at every whole-word place, a hyphen joining words; the typed places widened over a name they partly overlap. 10.20 keeps both and adds the marks to the boundaries.
- **10.18 (merged):** the question when nothing is left to review leads to [A] through the same `get_text_input`, so a name typed there is composed too.
- **10.19 and 10.17 (approved, not merged):** the forms rule across capitals (and accents dropped in capitals, Lionel's 10.19 Q3) and its desktop twin. Both rebase on 10.20 and add a decomposed-accent guard case (Ordering).
- **10.12 (merged):** one ERASURE entry per deleted row, no name; 10.20 keeps the loop for every form erased.
- **10.7 (merged):** the (text, type) key and the conversion on first open, the precedent for option 2; its unique key is why a name saved both ways cannot be rewritten into one form.
- **The PO's sweep** (`r1019/po/nfd`): the nine paths, the input types, the Word-made files; its probes look for a name in the source's own form, so on the fixed code their decomposed sources need a stand-in that looks for both forms (Task 7.4).

### Project Structure Notes

The composed form and the mark rule live in one small module of `utils/`, beside the readers that use it first, with no import of the package, so the readers, `validation/`, `core/` and `gui/` can all import it. `former_keys` lives beside `mapping_key`, whose old steps it reapplies; the repository imports it (`core/mapping_key.py` imports only `utils/`). [Source: architecture/12-unified-project-structure.md] No structural conflict.

### Technical Constraints

- Type hints on every public function; absolute imports; `mypy` strict. [Source: architecture/19-coding-standards.md#19.1]
- Quality commands: `black --check`, `ruff check`, `mypy gdpr_pseudonymizer/`, `pytest`. [Source: architecture/19-coding-standards.md#19.0]
- **Never log sensitive data:** no new log line holds a name; option 2's line holds counts only; the redaction keeps more names out (T22). [Source: architecture/19-coding-standards.md#19.1, rule 3]
- No flag, schema or file-format change; local and deterministic (Product Constraint 1): Python's own `unicodedata`.

### Testing

[Source: architecture/16-testing-strategy.md#16.2; pytest.ini; .github/workflows/ci.yaml]

- New tests as in the Test Plan: the real readers, the real CLI, the real desktop paths; detection replaced below the reader (T3 by the real regex layer, T4 the real detector).
- Tests first, strict xfail with `raises=AssertionError`, preconditions with `pytest.fail`, proven on every CI leg (AC12).
- Unmarked but T4, xdist-safe; no wall-clock assert; no sleep; no font file.
- Assertions on the readers' text, the written files, the stored rows, the lines shown and the ERASURE entries.
- No test reads or writes the user's settings file.

### Effort

**1-1.5 weeks** (the epic's estimate): the product change is broad but shallow (the SM's prototype v1.1: 18 product files, 186 lines added and 63 removed); the tests (57, a fifth on the desktop), the existing-test edits that 10.16's round-2 files need, the accuracy run, and Lionel's three questions are the larger part, then the Codex review and QA's end-to-end checks.

(5) Q1 (`custom_pseudonym.py:560`-`:568`): the rows of the type whose name is one of `saved_name_forms(name, type)`; the refusal names the pseudonym of the row whose form ranks first.
