## QA Results

### Review Date: 2026-10-22

### Reviewed By: Quinn (Test Architect)

**Scope:** PR #111 at head `87cd011` (branch `story/10.20-accents-one-form`, on `main` `3114e9e`, which holds 10.12 to 10.16 and 10.18), pre-merge (Task 7, G2).
- Commits read: tests first `c7e0818`, fix `1559488`, then `87cd011` (CHANGELOG and Dev Agent Record), on the story and epic docs commits.
- QA's runners, probes and logs are in `rgpd-analysis/r1020/qa/` and are not committed. Runs went through `.bat` runners with `cmd.exe` and the main Poetry environment (Python 3.11.9, pytest 9.1.1); test paths are grouped by directory. `USERPROFILE` and `HOME` were `r1020/qa/home`, and `gui.config._config_path` was patched.
- `C:\Users\devea\.gdpr-pseudo.yaml` was never opened (2026-10-14 13:27, 270 bytes). Nothing under `held_out/`, `final_exam/` or `tests/accuracy/` was opened: QA's worktree of `c7e0818` excludes `tests/test_corpus` and `tests/accuracy`, and its suite run ignores the six files that read the corpus. The accuracy artifact was compared by its score lines and by the leaf field names of its JSON only.

**Gate: CONCERNS** (quality score 90). Everything the story fixes holds on real output.
- **What holds:**
  - every reader, typed entry and saved-name lookup;
  - Lionel's Qa, Qb and Qc;
  - « Mme Zo » and the « Me » leak, gone with the real detector;
  - G2 and G1;
  - CI green at the first attempt on every leg of both code commits and the head.
- **The concern (SEARCH-001, medium):** rows an earlier version saved with lost letters are reached by reuse and by `delete-mapping`, but the three searches with the person's full name do not list them. AC3 and Lionel's Qa wording promise that the searches reach both kinds of old row.
- **Also:**
  - six behaviours that hold but no committed test pins (TEST-001, low);
  - the PR body still describes the tests-first draft (DOC-001, low).

### Code Quality Assessment (Task 7.1, the diff against the Fix Design)

- **One module.** `utils/text_form.py` (new, imports nothing of the package) provides:
  - `composed` (NFC);
  - `is_mark`;
  - `mark_class`, every combining mark of the running Python, cached;
  - `word_pattern`, `not_after_word` and `not_before_word`, which keep 10.15's hyphens.
- **The readers.**
  - `read_file` (text branch), `read_pdf` (the extracted text), `read_docx`, `read_excel` and `read_csv` return composed text.
  - Each cell of `read_excel_structured` and `read_csv_structured` is composed. Sheet names and cell references are unchanged.
- **The typed texts.**
  - Composed: `get_text_input` (every [A], [E], [C], [T] text); the `delete-mapping` name; the `list-mappings` search; `search_entities` (`list-entities`); the desktop correction dialog; the database screen and its worker; the review's filter. The custom pseudonym dialog is composed through `mapping_key`.
  - Not composed, as AC13 requires: passphrases, paths, `--reason` and the confirmation word. No change in `data/database.py`, `cli/passphrase.py` or the passphrase dialog.
- **The saved names.**
  - `mapping_key` composes first.
  - `former_keys` returns, in order: the key's steps on the decomposed text; the decomposed key; the key with « Maître » in front (three case forms, every type) and with « à » or « À » in front (LOCATION only). The words sit in two constants with the comment the story asks for.
  - The repository looks up `_name_forms` (composed, the former keys, the name as given) in one `IN` query in `find_by_full_name_and_type`, `_query_by_full_name` (and so `find_all_by_full_name` and `delete_entities_by_full_name`) and `find_by_component`.
  - Rule A: the composed form ranks first.
  - `import-mappings` saves composed names. No row is rewritten, no signature or schema changes.
- **The four word cuts:** `_WORD = word_pattern()`; the boundaries of `places_of`; `_locate_modified_span` skips a match that starts on a mark or ends just before one; the name run of `_sanitize_error_message` takes the marks.
- **Qc's line** byte for byte. The story's byte check gives `blocks=30 matched=30`, with Qc's line (count 2) a MATCH.
- **Untouched:** `nlp/`, `resources/`, `utils/french_patterns.py`, `utils/__init__.py`, `utils/logger.py`, `gdpr_pseudonymizer/__init__.py`, `.github/`, `pyproject.toml` and `poetry.lock` (empty diff). Nothing changed outside File Locations.

### G2: the tests first, on the unfixed code

QA's worktree is `r1020/qa/wt_c7e0818`. `git diff 3114e9e c7e0818 -- gdpr_pseudonymizer` is empty, so the code under test is `main`'s.
- **`--runxfail` on both new files:** 58 failed, 9 passed, each failure at the dev's line, no "PRE:".

### End to end on real output (Task 7.4)

QA's probes in `r1020/qa/probes/` drive the real Typer app, the real readers and the real desktop workers. Detection is replaced below the reader by a stand-in that finds each name in both spellings in the text it receives, except where the real detector runs. Decomposed forms are built at run time.

- **Command line**, `test_q20_cli.py`: 27 passed and 1 skipped (the PDF probe is decomposed only). On `main`'s code, 19 fail.
  - **Readers.** `process` on `.txt`, `.md`, `.docx`, a PDF that draws its accents apart (pdfplumber gives the marks apart), `.xlsx` and `.csv`, each decomposed and composed:
    - the written file has no combining mark;
    - the name is replaced;
    - « été », « témoin » and « présent » are kept, composed;
    - one row, composed;
    - `read_file` on the PDF alone returns composed text.
  - **Batch.** `batch --workers 1` on a decomposed and a composed file (`.txt`, `.docx`, `.csv`): one pseudonym in both outputs, one row.
  - **Both forms in one document:** « 1 unique », one pseudonym.
  - **[A].** Composed in a decomposed document, the reverse, and both decomposed: no « not found », both places replaced, one row.
  - **[E].** Typed in the other form: nothing of the name in clear.
  - **[C].** Typed decomposed: written and stored composed.
  - **Searches and deletion.** `list-entities` and `list-mappings --search` and `delete-mapping`, typed in the other form: listed and erased.
  - **Not composed:**
    - a database made with a decomposed passphrase opens with it and refuses its composed spelling;
    - a folder and files with decomposed names are read and written under those exact names;
    - `--reason` is stored decomposed, as typed.
  - **A text without accents:** written with the same bytes as `main`'s code writes (SHA-256 equal).
- **Desktop**, `test_q20_desktop.py`: 20 passed, 1 skipped.
  - **Item 3, the single document through the real reader.** `.txt`, `.md`, `.docx` and the drawn-apart PDF go through the real `DetectionWorker` and `read_file`, only `_detect_and_filter_entities` replaced:
    - the review's own text holds no mark, and the row is the composed name;
    - the written file is composed, the name replaced, the other accented words kept;
    - one row, composed.
  - **Dialogs.** The correction dialog typed composed or decomposed on a decomposed `.txt` and `.docx`: no « Quentrel » in clear. « Changer le pseudonyme » typed decomposed: written and stored composed.
  - **Filter.** The review's filter typed decomposed lists the name.
  - **Old databases.** A v2.2.0 database (name saved decomposed, or both ways) with a decomposed `.txt` and `.docx`: the old pseudonym (rule A), the stored rows unchanged.
  - **Database screen.** The search finds the decomposed row typed in either form.
  - **Batch of three with review.** A decomposed `.txt`, a decomposed `.docx` and a mixed `.txt`: one row, one pseudonym in the three outputs, composed.
- **The PO's nine paths.** QA ran the PO's probes from a copy (`r1020/qa/po_probes/`) whose stand-in finds both spellings; 32 passed (they print). Every decomposed case is written like its composed copy:
  - paths 2 to 4: [A], [E] and grouping, « 1 unique » for the mixed text;
  - path 7: the same pseudonym, `process` twice and batch;
  - path 8: deletion with its entry;
  - the inputs probe: every format read composed.
  - **With the real detector** (the PO's ten sentences as one document): the decomposed copy gives the same 10 rows and the same names left in clear as the composed copy (« Éloria Vórlan » missed by both). « Mme Zo » is gone (« Mme Mahaut »).
  - Path 9 is 10.17's and 10.19's (not in this tree).
- **The real detector**, `test_q20_real.py`, decomposed against composed:
  - « Signé : Mme Zórbalia » gives « Mme <pseudonym> »;
  - « Le contrat avec Mélora Quentrel a signé. » on a fresh database gives « Le contrat avec <pseudonym> a signé. », the row « Mélora Quentrel » composed;
  - the same rows as the composed copy.
  - On `main`'s code the decomposed copies write « Delaunaýrbalia » and « Mélora Quentrel a Victoria Teixeira ».

### Performance (item 7)

QA's probe `test_q20_perf.py` ran on a database of 2,000 saved names, head against `main`'s code, three timings each, two runs.
- **500 lookups by name:** 0.156 to 0.178 s against 0.113 to 0.120 s. That is about 0.1 ms more per lookup, the SM's measurement.
- **`finalize_document` of a 50-name document:**
  - all new: 0.281 to 0.321 s against 0.271 to 0.306 s;
  - all known: 0.220 to 0.266 s against 0.226 to 0.255 s.
- So about a hundredth of a second or less per 50-name document, as Lionel's Qa said.
- **The dev's local performance suite:** 7, 3 and 4 passed (+3 skipped); benchmarks 0.348, 0.613, 0.941 and 0.555 s. The run on `main` after the merge is the comparison.

  - **Why it matters:** AC3 lists the three searches among the comparisons and says « So a database written by v2.2.0 or by today's code still matches, with both kinds of old row »; Lionel's Qa says « each time the app looks for a saved name, it also looks for the ways an earlier version could have saved it ». The CHANGELOG says such names « are still found ».
  - **Reproduction:** `test_q20_olddbs.py::test_old_row_found_by_search_with_the_bare_name` on `po/dbs/v220_melora.db`, and the desktop `test_d5`.
  - **Suggested:** either the searches also try the search text's former forms (`former_keys`) as substrings (four places, with tests), or the PO and Lionel accept the limit and the CHANGELOG says the searches find such names by a part of the name.
- **TEST-001 (low).** Six behaviours that hold on real output are pinned by no committed test:

  - **M19:** `_locate_modified_span`'s mark check. This one is reachable only with a mark that has no composed letter, which French does not use.
  - QA's probes `test_q20_cli.py` e7 and e8, `test_q20_desktop.py` d2, `test_q20_component.py` and `test_q20_gaps.py` g1 are models.
- **DOC-001 (low).** The PR body describes the tests-first draft; the PR is still a draft. Task 6.5 and 6.7 are the orchestrator's.
- **OBS-001 (info).** The dev's smell in 10.16's batch test is harmless (M35); backlog as the dev says.

### NFR Validation

- **Security: CONCERNS.** No name or part of a name is left in clear on any path QA ran, in either form. SEARCH-001 makes a stored person invisible to the full-name search, the tool a data-protection officer would use to answer an access request. The row is still erased by name.
- **Performance: PASS.** About 0.1 ms more per lookup; about a hundredth of a second per 50-name document.
- **Reliability: PASS.** No stored row is rewritten. CI was green at attempt 1 on every run.
- **Maintainability: PASS.** One module for the form and the marks; the repository's signatures are unchanged. TEST-001 lists six unpinned behaviours.

### SEARCH-001 on real output (item 1)

QA's probe `test_q20_search.py` (new) copies each of QA's 24 old databases and searches the person's full name, composed, through the four searches:
- `list-entities --search` and `list-mappings --search` (the real Typer app);
- the desktop database screen inline (the database's own rows, 200 or fewer), the search typed composed and typed decomposed;
- the same screen with 250 more rows, so the search goes through `DatabaseWorker` and `_on_search_complete`;
- `DatabaseWorker("search", …)` run alone with its `finished` signal captured.

| Code | Databases where every search lists the row | Missed |
|---|---|---|
| `457ed11` | 24 of 24, on all six paths | none |
| `87cd011` | 17 of 24 | the same 7 as round 1, on all six paths: `v220_melora`, `base_melora`, `v220_jerome`, `base_jerome`, `v220_maitre_melora`, `base_maitre_melora`, `v220_mangled` |

- Round 1's probes `test_old_row_found_by_search_with_the_bare_name` (24 of 24) and `test_d5_database_screen_search` pass at the head.
- So the CHANGELOG's « are still found, reused and erased » now holds for the searches too.

### G13 and AC13: clean databases (item 2)

QA's clean database holds 13 rows. Eleven are composed, as today's code writes them: « Éloria Quentrel », « Mélora Quentrel », « Lora Quentrel », « Jérôme Quentrel », « Zórbalia Quentrel », « Vorlan Mestrix », « Mélanie Vorlan », the places « Zórbaville » and « Lyon Zórbaville », the organisations « Quentrel Zórbal SARL » and « Café à Zórbaville ». Two test the edges: « Zox » + lone U+0301 + « rbalia Quentrel » (a mark with no composed letter, which NFC keeps), and an organisation « Maître Vorlan », which today's code cannot write.

28 searches, each typed composed and, where it differs, decomposed (48 lines), went through the four paths at `457ed11` and at `87cd011`. The searches were: « Mme Éloria », « Éloria », « Maître Éloria », « Monsieur Vorlan », « Mélora », « Mélora Quentrel », « Mé », « Me », « Mè », « é », « Jérôme », « Jérôme Quentrel », « Quentrel », « à Zórbaville », « à », « À », « Maître », « Mme », « Zórbaville », « zórbaville », « MÉLORA », « Mélanie », « Zox », « lora », « Lora Quentrel », « Café », « Me Vorlan », « Maître Vorlan ».
- **42 of 48 lines are identical**, on all four paths. Among them:
  - « Mme Éloria » lists no « Éloria Quentrel » (G13);
  - « à », « À » and « à Zórbaville » list the same rows as today: the « à » forms reach LOCATION rows only;
  - « Maître », « é », « Mè », « Zox », « lora » and « Quentrel » list the same rows;
  - « Mélora » does not list « Lora Quentrel ».
- **4 lines differ on the « Maître Vorlan » row:** « Monsieur Vorlan », « Me », « Mme » and « Me Vorlan » now list it.
  - A search tries « maître » + its key: « Mme Éloria » looks for « maître éloria », as the Fix Design says. A title alone has an empty key, so it looks for « maître ».
  - Today's code never writes such a row: `mapping_key` strips every title anywhere in the text, for every type (« Cabinet Maître Vorlan » (ORG) is keyed « Cabinet Vorlan »).
  - So these lines move only on a database holding an old row with a decomposed « Maître ». Listing that row is the design.
- **2 lines differ on the lone-mark row: « Mé », typed either way, now lists « Zox » + lone U+0301 + « rbalia Quentrel » (SEARCH-002, low).**
  - Decomposed, « Mé » is « Me » + a mark. The title pattern takes « Me » off, so one of its former forms is the lone U+0301 alone, and it is a substring of every name holding that mark with no composed letter.
  - « Mél » gives a lone U+0301 + « l », and so on, so a longer search narrows at once.
  - Today's code writes such a name as it is (NFC has no composed letter for x + U+0301, nor for a letter with two marks such as e with a dot below and an acute).
  - Nothing is written, changed or erased by it. A search typed while the screen filters (300 ms) may show that extra row for a moment.
  - French names do not have such a letter. Names from other languages in a French document can.
- **Probe `test_q20_search_loc.py` (new): the forms of the row's own type.** The two kinds differ on an old row only for a search led by a preposition.
  - « de Mézorville » finds a LOCATION row saved as a lone U+0301 + « zorville », on all four paths; so does « Mézorville ».
  - « de Mélora » does not list a PERSON row saved as a lone U+0301 + « lora Quentrel », which « Mélora Quentrel » lists.
  - « de Zélvorbix » does not list « Zélvorbix ».
  - On `87cd011`, the three rows that should be found are missed.

### Search cost (item 5)

QA's probe `test_q20_searchcost.py` (new) uses 10,000 invented names: a third saved decomposed, as v2.2.0 did, and a tenth of them places. It compared the head with `87cd011` in two runs each.
- **The desktop worker's filter (median of 9):**
  - head: 0.0155 to 0.0231 s per search;
  - `87cd011`: 0.0065 to 0.0089 s.
  - So about a hundredth of a second more per search, as the SM measured (0.015 to 0.019 s against 0.006 s). The slowest is « Mélora Quentrel », which has the most forms: 0.0227 to 0.0231 s.
- **`list-entities`' `search_entities` on a database of 10,000 rows (median of 3):**
  - head: 0.437 to 0.494 s;
  - `87cd011`: 0.444 to 0.478 s.
  - Reading the rows alone (`find_all`) takes 0.417 to 0.467 s, so decryption dominates and the difference is within the noise.
- **Searches without accents list the same rows on both:** « Qua » 3,448 rows, « vor » 4,300 rows.

### Findings, round 2

- **SEARCH-001: resolved** in `0b751e5` (item 1).
- **TEST-001: resolved** (item 3). G8, G10, G11 and G12 catch M08, M25, M27 and M33. G9 catches the search typed decomposed and read raw. M23's own edit now reaches only the pseudonym half (TEST-002). M19 is in the backlog by the ruling.
- **SEARCH-002 (low): one false widening on a clean database.**
  - **What happens:** a search typed « Mé », either way, now also lists a saved name that holds an accent mark with no composed letter, such as « Zox » + lone U+0301 + « rbalia Quentrel ».
  - **Why:** decomposed, « Mé » is « Me » + a mark. The title pattern takes « Me » off, so one of the search's former forms is the lone U+0301 alone.
  - **Which searches:** any search led by « Mé », « Mè », « Mê » or « Më », or « Mmé ». « Mél » gives a lone U+0301 + « l », so a longer search narrows at once.
  - **Who can be affected:** today's code saves such a name as it is, because NFC has no composed letter for it (x + acute; a letter with two marks). French names do not use such letters; names from other languages can.
  - **Why low:** nothing is written or erased. The desktop list may show the extra row while the search is being typed.
  - **AC13 says** a typed name and a saved name written the usual way are « compared … exactly as today ».
  - **Reproduction:** `test_q20_search.py::test_s2_clean_database_results`, the « Mé » lines (`logs/s2_head.txt` against `logs/s2_base.txt`).
  - **Suggested:** either the dev makes a former form that begins with a mark count only where that mark does not follow a letter in the saved name, with a test, or the PO and Lionel accept the corner and put it in the backlog. Every old row QA has keeps its lone mark at the start of the name or after a space, so the first option keeps all 24 found. The other differing lines on QA's clean database are on the organisation « Maître Vorlan », a row today's code cannot write (titles are stripped everywhere, for every type), so they show only on old rows, as the design intends.
- **TEST-002 (low): no committed test pins two behaviours that hold at the head.**
  - **The forms of the row's type:** QA mutants F03 ×4, F04 ×4, F10 and F11 survive all 636 committed tests. QA's S2 and S4 catch each.
  - **The pseudonym half of `list-entities --search` and `list-mappings --search` typed decomposed:** P1 and M23 as written survive. QA's S5 catches them.
  - **Suggested (backlog):** a LOCATION twin of G13 (« à Zórbaville » lists no « Zórbaville »); S4's « de Mézorville » row; a pseudonym typed decomposed in T17's searches.
- **DOC-001 (low), still open:** the PR body describes the tests-first draft, and the PR is still a draft (Tasks 6.5 and 6.7).
- **OBS-001 and OBS-002 (info):** as in round 1.
- **OBS-003 (info).**
  - The clause `or former == search` in `search_forms` never changes the result (mutant F02).
  - The PO's desktop batch probe (`test_nfd_desk.py`, three batch cases) times out on both `87cd011` and the head. Its stand-in detector looks the document up by the text as written on disk, and the reader now composes it. This is a probe artefact; QA's own batch probe d6 passes.
- **Not checked by QA:** the read-only Codex review (Task 6.3, the orchestrator's step, on the final head).

### Lookup cost (item 6)

QA's probe `test_q20_lookupcost.py` (new) uses 10,000 invented names, a third saved decomposed and a tenth of them places. It compared the head with `186972b`, two runs each.
- **500 lookups of saved names:**
  - head: 0.226 to 0.255 s;
  - `186972b`: 0.165 to 0.176 s.
  - About 0.06 to 0.08 s more, roughly 0.15 ms per lookup. The SM measured 0.203 against 0.144 s, the same ratio.
- **500 lookups of absent names:** 0.172 to 0.192 s, against 0.121 s.
- **A name of 6 words, 4 of them accented:** 0.81 to 0.86 ms per lookup, against 0.26 ms. The SM measured 0.7 ms.
- **`finalize_document` of a 50-name document on the 10,000 rows:** 1.01 to 1.15 s, against 1.03 to 1.11 s, so no measurable difference.
- So a 50-name document waits a hundredth of a second or less more, as the story says.

  - **What happens:** a custom pseudonym equal to the full name of a person whose old row was cut at its end is accepted and written. « Vorlan Zorbôme » beside an old « Vorlan Zorbô » row is written « Vorlan Zorbôme valide. ».
  - **Why:** Q-C2 compares with the lookup forms, and Lionel's answer A leaves the "cut" keys out of the lookups.
  - **Why it could change:** a refusal cannot reach the wrong person's row, so Q-C2 alone could also try the "cut" keys. The cost is that it may also refuse « Vorlan Zorbôme » beside a row that really was « Vorlan Zorbô ».
  - **Rare:** of the app's 106 accented names, only « Jérôme » is cut at its end, alone or as a surname (the SM's measurement).

- **IMPORT-001 (low, older than this story).**
  - **What happens:** `import-mappings --skip-duplicates` does not see an old row with a kept title or preposition (« Maître Éloria Quentrel », decomposed) as a duplicate of the clean « Éloria Quentrel » row in the target. It adds a second row.
  - **Why it is harmless:** the bare name keeps the clean row's pseudonym (rule A), `delete-mapping` erases both (two ERASURE entries), and the custom-pseudonym checks see both.
  - **When it started:** the same on `186972b`.
  - **Suggested (backlog):** import's duplicate check also tries `mapping_key` of the source text.
- **TEST-003 (low).** K08, K15, K16 and W4 survive the committed tests (item 5). QA's probes `test_q20_r3extra.py` X1, X2 and X3 and `test_q20_codex.py` P2 are models.

### Findings, round 4

- **Resolved this round:**
  - Codex review 2's two P0s and its P1 (item 1);
  - SEARCH-002 (« Mé », item 3);
  - CUSTOM-001, now refused as Lionel answered (item 2).
- **IMPORT-002 (high, a regression of round 3).**
  - **What happens:** `import-mappings` composes an old row the old version glued (« Je » + U+0301 + « ro » + U+0302 + « Quentrel » becomes « JérôQuentrel »). The row then holds no mark, no « Maître » and no leading « à », so `is_legacy` is false and Q-C2 and the four searches never repair it.
  - **Effect:** [C] « Jérôme Quentrel » (also « Zorbôme Quentrel », « Zorbôme de la Quentrel ») for another person is accepted and written. `list-entities` and `list-mappings --search` with the full name list nothing.
  - **Against round 3:** `56feb3d` refused and listed all of these (its forward forms included the composed glued key, Codex P1-3).
  - **Who it touches:** « Jérôme » before a surname is the glued case of the app's own list; T38 imports this kind of row for the lookups only.
  - **The CHANGELOG's** « A custom pseudonym equal to a name an earlier version saved wrongly … is refused. » does not hold for such rows.
  - **Reproduction:** `test_q20_r4.py::test_r4_imported_old_row_check_and_search[v220-jerome_full]` (and `zorbome_quentrel`, `de_la`, both trees); `test_q20_codex.py::test_p13_imported_old_row[*-zorbome_quentrel]` (the search).
  - **Suggested:** Q-C2 and the searches also compare each row with no mark through the composed forward keys, as at `56feb3d`, or another way the dev chooses; with a committed test (import a glued row, then [C] refused and the full-name search listing it).
- **REPAIR-001 (medium, AC13 and Lionel's Q-C2 « Whole names only »).**
  - **What happens:** `_droppable` (S4) takes any word whose fold is « maitre ». « Maitre » and « MAITRE » without the circumflex (a surname, often written without accents in capitals) were never matched by the old pattern, so today's code saves them in clean databases.
  - **Effect:** a custom pseudonym holding the rest of such a name is refused, which `56feb3d` accepted, and the searches list the name by its other words.
  - **Reproduction:** `test_q20_r4.py::test_r4_clean_maitre_surname_real_output` and `test_r4_clean_maitre_word` (printed; compare with `56feb3d`).
  - **Suggested:** S4 only for a word « Maître » with its circumflex (`composed(word).casefold() == "maître"`), and a G-test with « MAITRE ».
- **SEARCH-003 (low).** On old databases, a search of a few letters can list a lone-mark row through a put-back title: « Léo » lists a lone U+0301 + « orvan Quentrel », through « Mllé » + « orvan ». Nothing is written or erased.
- **TEST-004 (low).** L05, L11, L14, L15 and L16 survive the committed tests (item 6).
- **Unchanged from earlier rounds:**
  - IMPORT-001 (low): an old titled row imported beside its clean row is a second row;
  - TEST-002 and TEST-003 (low, backlog);
  - DOC-001 (low, open).
- **Info:**
  - the mixed row with a decomposed « Maître » is not reached by a lookup (the stated limit); the check refuses its name;
  - with the search read as typed, « de Mézorville » no longer lists a place saved as a lone U+0301 + « zorville »; « Mézorville » does (the design);
  - QA's probes that pinned round 3's behaviour (`test_p02_cli_cut_name_accepted_under_a`, `test_s4_row_type_forms[de Mézorville]`) fail by design now.
- **Not checked by QA:** the third read-only Codex review (the orchestrator's).
